# Paper overview

Running list of new papers that matter for the engine. Newest first per topic.

**Last research: 2026-11-20 (week 47)** (tool scan `tools/papers/scan.py`, topics v1: complete coverage 2026-10-24 to 2026-11-17 for arXiv and 2026-10-24 to 2026-11-19 for GitHub PRs and issues, see [papers-coverage.md](papers-coverage.md); arXiv 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19 are still open (the API lists a date only after 3 days) and are searched again from 2026-11-21; before 2026-10-24 the backfill is ticket #120 part 3)

**Rule:** the weekly search runs only through `tools/papers/scan.py` ([README](../tools/papers/README.md)). First run
`python3 tools/papers/scan.py coverage`; close every gap up to yesterday before anything else. Coverage per run:
[papers-coverage.md](papers-coverage.md); every hit with its decision: [papers-screened.tsv](papers-screened.tsv)
(screening scheme in the tool README); open PRs under watch: [papers-watch.tsv](papers-watch.tsv), re-checked with
`scan.py watch`. Entries here give source, authors' numbers or own measurement, abstract-only or read, code link and
evaluation hardware; status `new -> read -> transferable -> tested`, or `rejected (reason, measurement link)`.

**Relevance:** high = directly transferable to gfx1200 + MoE offload; medium = idea
transferable; low = context only.
**Status:** new → read → transferable → tested (with a reference to `measurements/`).

First state 2026-10-22: agent research from search results, **papers not yet
read**, numbers are the authors' claims.

Second search 2026-10-22 (gaps in quantization, linear/sparse attention, speculation, RDNA):
also **agent research, papers not read**. Numbers from abstracts, HTML versions
and blogs. arXiv IDs opened on arxiv.org unless "ID unchecked" or "no arXiv"
is stated. "(n auth.)" = number of authors.

## Megakernels / persistent kernels

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09 | [ForgeMegakernel, arXiv 2609.12379](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12379) | Stages: instruction stream per SM, dependency counters, LDS buffer pool. Qwen3-8B/H100: even with counters 218–304 µs waiting per token (load imbalance); without counters 29.6 % MBU, with 35 % (agent claim 2026-10-25, paper text) | **high** | Finding confirmed: here barrier 1 ms, imbalance 7.3 → 4.4 ms per pass after redistribution (measurements/2026-10-25-barriers) |
| 2026-09 | [Cohere: Megakernel Serving Engine](https://cohere.com/blog/megakernels) (blog) | Decode megakernel on H100, 1.25–1.41× vs. vLLM; vLLM reaches only 39 % SoL. Counters in device memory per task (wait, count, signal), spin with nanosleep, host zeroes per step; dependency placement 1–2 % slower (agent claim 2026-10-25) | high | new |
| 2026-06 | [AutoMegaKernel, arXiv 2606.09682](https://papers.cool/arxiv/2606.09682) | HF model → one persistent kernel, static check for deadlocks | medium | new |
| 2026-05 | [Ada-MK, arXiv 2605.11581](https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2605.11581) | Kernel launches alone 14.6 % of the time in decode; fusion into one persistent kernel | medium | new |
| 2026-04 | [Fleet, arXiv 2604.15379](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15379) | Megakernels on multi-die GPUs | low | new |
| 2025-12 | [Mirage Persistent Kernel, arXiv 2512.22219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22219v1) | Compiler + runtime for one kernel per inference, up to 1.7× less latency. Event counters u64 monotonic (threshold n · iteration, no reset), wait with acquire load + nanosleep, signal with release add (agent claim 2026-10-25, `persistent_kernel.cuh` in branch mpk) | high | new |
| 2025-05 | [Hazy Research: No Bubbles](https://hazyresearch.stanford.edu/blog/2025-05-27-no-bubbles) (blog) | Llama-1B megakernel: counter per instruction, MLP intermediate value in 4 pieces with a counter each; B200 600 µs per pass, of which 250 µs storing/synchronizing/loading activations (agent claim 2026-10-25) | medium | new |

## MoE offloading / expert cache

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09 | [SeqMoE, arXiv 2609.12978](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12978) | Seq2seq prediction of the experts across steps and layers, eviction by "probabilistic Belady"; 96.97 % hits at 45 % resident experts (agent claim 2026-10-25, abstract only) | medium (trained predictor) | new |
| 2026-08 | [Reproducible Evaluation of MoE Expert Caching, arXiv 2608.07911](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07911) | LFRU best causal policy (12/13); 84–97 % of the gap to Belady is knowledge of future use; learned next-use predictor worse than LFRU; sequential replay inflates LRU/LFRU by ~27–29 % (agent claim 2026-10-25) | **high** | confirms our findings: LRFU in the server without gain (measurements/2026-10-24-misses) |
| 2026-07 | [DraftExpert, arXiv 2607.24434](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24434) | Draft stops when it would need too many new experts; preload hits 86–88 %, 1.45× (agent claim 2026-10-25) | medium | similar to IE_SPEC_CUT, discarded |
| 2026-02 | [DALI, arXiv 2602.03495](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03495) | local PCs: replacement by score over a sliding token window, preloading via residual (agent claim 2026-10-25) | medium | new |
| 2025-11 | [In-depth Analysis on Caching and Pre-fetching in MoE Offloading, arXiv 2511.05814](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.05814) | Preloading can hurt if it hits something useful when evicting (agent claim 2026-10-25) | **high** | tested: protect predicted, already loaded experts before preloading (`IE_PRE_KEEP`), decode +0.8–0.9 % (measurements/2026-10-25-bit-exact) |
| 2025-11 | [Specter / MoE-SpeQ, arXiv 2511.14102](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14102) | quantized draft in VRAM, its routing starts the transfers (agent claim 2026-10-25) | low (DFlash is dense, no routing) | new |
| 2025-10 | [SP-MoE, arXiv 2510.10302](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10302) | Preloading and pipeline matched to speculation, 1.07–3.5× TPOT (agent claim 2026-10-25) | medium | new |
| 2025-09 | [Cache Management for MoE LLMs, arXiv 2509.02408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02408) | "layered paging": fixed slots per layer unboundedly competitive, layer-aware LRU with wandering slots (agent claim 2026-10-25) | medium | tested 2026-10-25: unequal slots +0.6–1.4 points online, not worth the rework |
| 2026-08 | [SPICE, arXiv 2608.21240](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21240) | Misses by confidence: fetch, CPU exact or substitute (shared expert + low-rank surrogate); up to 3.12× TPOT, but GSM8K −3.4, HumanEval −2.4 to −3.9 (agent claim 2026-10-24, emergentmind read) | medium (quality loss) | new |
| 2026-07 | [ECOSPEC, arXiv 2607.12696](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12696) | Draft tree by path probability / new experts, lossless; Qwen3-235B union 23.7 → 20.5 experts, 1.22 → 1.36× (agent claim 2026-10-24) | **high** | tested as trimming the verify pass (`IE_SPEC_CUT`): not worth it, one token is worth ~14 ms, its experts ~3 ms |
| 2026 | Research Square rs-11268552 "Which Expert Misses Are Worth Waiting For?" | Qwen3-Next-80B, 25 % experts in VRAM: replacing all misses +5.7 % PPL, fetching only top-1 or weight ≥ 0.15 +0.35 % (emulation; snippet only, agent claim 2026-10-24) | **high** (same model family) | tested (`IE_MISS_DROP`): w = 0.1 → +1 % NLL, +3–9 % decode; off |
| 2026-01 | [FlashMoE, arXiv 2601.17063](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17063) | Qwen3-30B traces: LRU ~73 %, Belady ~86 % hits; 34 % of the experts evicted by LRU needed again within 5 steps (snippet, agent claim 2026-10-24) | medium | tested: here LRU 85–87 %, Belady 93–95 % (90 slots) |
| 2025-11 | [BuddyMoE, arXiv 2511.10054](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10054) | Replace misses with a co-activated buddy in VRAM; +5.8–10.3 % tok/s, ARC −5.4 % (agent claim 2026-10-24, HTML read) | low | new |
| 2026-10 | [ik_llama.cpp PR #2633, #2676](https://github.com/ikawrakow/ik_llama.cpp) (`ggml/src/iqk/iqk_gemm_iquants.cpp`) | AVX-512 TG kernels for IQ3_XXS/IQ2_S/IQ3_S: scales as integers per 256 (Q8_K), sign masks from parity, 4-row repack; Zen 4 up to 1.35×; table lookups still individually, gather slower on Zen 4 (agent claim 2026-10-25, code read) | **high** | partly adopted (zmm pairs, parity): whole experts ~1.35×, measurements/2026-10-25-cpu-experts |
| 2026-06 | [arXiv 2606.10493 (OSDI '26)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10493) | AVX-512 GEMV for experts on the CPU: scale only after accumulation, 4–5× faster (abstract) | medium: CPU experts here only ~19 GB/s of 58 | new |
| 2026-10 | [MoE-CORE, arXiv 2610.01950](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01950) | MoE prefill with experts in RAM: two whole layer buffers, next layer loads during the current one (agent claim, research/18) | **high** | tested: layer-wise prefill, private notes |
| 2026-08 | [FreeToken, arXiv 2608.16157](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16157) | Preload a whole layer instead of after routing, shared slot pool for prefill and decode (agent claim, research/18) | **high** | tested (loan slots) |
| 2025-10 | [Layered Prefill, arXiv 2510.08055](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08055) | Prefill layer-major instead of chunk-major: load experts once per layer instead of once per piece (agent claim, research/18) | **high** | tested: prefill 13K +7–11 %, bit-identical |
| 2026-07 | [DraftExpert, arXiv 2607.24434](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24434) | Speculation with experts in RAM: costs depend on newly loaded experts, not on the draft length; 1.45× decode, prefetch hits 86–88 % (1 auth.) | **high** | new |
| 2025-10 | [MoBiLE, arXiv 2510.12357](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12357v1) | Big-little experts without training, 1.6–1.7× on consumer GPU | medium | new |
| 2025-08 | [Importance-Driven Expert Scheduling, arXiv 2508.18983](https://arxiv.org/html/2508.18983v1) | Replace unimportant experts with similar, already cached ones; −48 % decode latency | medium (quality risk) | new |
| 2026-11 | [Tideway, arXiv 2611.14848](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14848) | VRAM and host RAM as one pool for MoE on consumer PCs, phase-aware layouts (prefill vs decode), CUDA-graph execution under expert paging; authors: 1.97x end-to-end, 2.40x TTFT, 1.61x decode against the fastest baseline; code github.com/Afterglow231/Tideway; full text and code read 2026-11-16 (RTX 5090, 251 GiB RAM capped 16-128 GiB, baselines SSD-LLaMA and FlashMoE re-implemented by the authors): dedup of VRAM and RAM copies, anchors against prefill scans worth measuring, decode graphs not applicable; verdicts in [57-tideway-comparison.md](57-tideway-comparison.md) | high | read, transferable (partly) |
| 2026-11 | [SlipStream, arXiv 2611.14776](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14776) | Demand-paced prefetch of the repeatable weight-page order for high-bandwidth flash (hardware proposal); relevant as an idea only: replay the known demand order to stage experts, bound the lead by the consumer; abstract only | low | new |
| 2026-11 | [LazyWeights, arXiv 2611.17108](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17108) | GPTQ tuned so the codes Huffman-compress (5.7-31.4 % smaller than the best llama.cpp format), decoded on demand by an ASIC unit; the byte saving needs hardware, but the code-skew observation applies to our IQ formats; abstract only | low | new |
| 2026-11 | [metalSPARSE, arXiv 2611.14545](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14545) | 2:4 sparse GEMV for Apple GPUs, 97 % of the byte-reduction speedup in decode (1.5 B model, M2/M4); prompt processing does not gain; Mac context only; abstract only | low | new |

## Speculative decoding

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10 | [vLLM: Adaptive Verification, online estimator](https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-14-adaptive-verification-online-estimator) (blog, PR #52228, #47808) | Acceptance per position σ(ω · logit q_max + β_i), fitted online by IRLS; raw q_max ECE 0.074 → 0.010; width by E[tokens] / step cost (agent claim 2026-10-25, blog read) | **high** | adopted (`IE_DRAFT_CALIB`, with step-size control and a probe every 32nd pass): server +0.9 % on average, measurements/2026-10-25-drafter-calibration |
| 2026-08 | [incoai/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-DFlash2](https://huggingface.co/incoai/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-DFlash2) (HF, citation 2026-08) + GGUF sidecars in [ggml-org/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF) (`dflash-…-Q8_0.gguf` 0.65 GB, `mtp-…-Q8_0.gguf` 4.14 GB) | **DFlash2 drafter for our model**, 0.6 B parameters, block size 4 (3 drafts, "limited by the QSA implementation"). Acceptance length at concurrency 1 against MTP: HumanEval 2.94 / 2.52, MBPP 3.06 / 2.65, MT-Bench 2.83 / 2.50; 1.63–1.82× against no speculation (GB300, SGLang with own patch). According to the model card, not re-measured | **high**: one drafter pass (0.65 GB) instead of 3 MTP steps (~0.7 GB each with LM head), higher acceptance. Invalidates the note "no drafter available" for DFlash below | new, 2026-10-23 |
| 2026-10 | [ReplaySSM, arXiv 2610.13778](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13778) | Instead of a state snapshot per draft buffer the inputs, rollback = pointer back; verifying 8 tokens costs 0.89–1.21× of one step instead of 4.66×. At B=1 only "a few percent". Incidentally: FP16 state with stochastic rounding ≈ FP32 (7 auth.) | **high**: replaces our 450 MiB rollback snapshots (k=3), provides precision data for open question 1 | new |
| 2026-08 | [Bole, arXiv 2608.01651](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01651) | Tree speculation through DeltaNet layers in closed form, only the chosen state is reconstructed; verification 3.4–7.7× faster, intermediate state 82–99× smaller, agent TPOT −49.9 % (12 auth.) | **high**: SuffixDecoding provides trees, our model has 36 DeltaNet layers | new |
| 2026-08 | [AcceptMoE, arXiv 2608.02989](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02989) | Verification loads the expert union of all tree nodes; limit the expert set by router demand: with offload 2.06×, H2D traffic −74–77 %, but −0.27 pp. accuracy (7 auth.) | **high** (lossy): exactly our case speculation + experts in RAM | new |
| 2026-07 | [SpecLA, arXiv 2607.16673](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16673) | GDN speculation: store compact factors from the verification, reconstruct the accepted state from them; 1.70× on H100, GDN-1.3B (8 auth.) | medium: alternative to ReplaySSM, only a small model measured | new |
| 2026-06 | [DFlash, arXiv 2602.06036](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06036) (2026-02, 3 auth.) + [LMSYS blog DFlash v2](https://www.lmsys.org/blog/2026-06-15-next-generation-speculative-decoding-dflash-v2) | Block-diffusion drafter proposes 16 tokens in one pass; Qwen3.5-397B-A17B at concurrency 1 on HumanEval 4.3× vs. base, 1.5× vs. native MTP (7 steps) | medium: no drafter for Qwen3.8 available (we would have to train one), verifying 16 tokens hits the expert union in RAM | new |
| 2026-05 | [Baseten: suffix automaton + MTP](https://www.baseten.co/blog/boosting-mtp-acceptance-rates-in-tensorrt-llm/) (blog, 2026-01, upd. 2026-05) | A long suffix hit overrides the MTP draft: on code edits +34 % acceptance length, production coding agents up to +40 % throughput; on reasoning text ≈ 0 hits | **high**: exactly our combination (SuffixDecoding + built-in MTP) for coding agents | new |
| 2026-05 | [Component-Aware Self-Spec, arXiv 2605.01106](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01106) | Negative result: draft only over the linear layers is accepted at 0.038 for k=2 on Qwen3.5 (sequential hybrid), at 0.68 on Falcon-H1 (parallel) (3 auth.) | low: saves a dead end (DeltaNet-only as drafter) | new |
| 2026-04 | [SpecMoE, arXiv 2604.10152](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10152) | Self-speculation without training for CPU-offloaded MoE, up to 4.30× throughput, DAC 2026 (5 auth.) | medium: target is rather batch throughput, not B=1 | new |
| 2025-10 | [SP-MoE, arXiv 2510.10302](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10302) | Draft pass predicts experts of the target, prefetch over PCIe parallel to the draft phase; 1.07–3.5× TPOT (5 auth.) | medium: the MTP draft could trigger the expert prefetch for verification | new |
| 2025-09 | [FastMTP, arXiv 2509.18362](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18362) | One MTP head recursively: untuned acceptance drops 70 → 10 → ~0 % over steps 1–3, after self-distillation 80/56/36 %, 2.03× (10 auth.) | medium: yardstick for our draft length; the Qwen TR reports instead 4.07 accepted tokens at 4 steps | new |
| 2024-11, NeurIPS 2025 | [SuffixDecoding, arXiv 2411.04975](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04975) | Suffix trees over prompt and earlier outputs, without a draft model; up to 5.3× on SWE-Bench agents | **high** | new |

## KV and prefix cache for agents

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09 | [When Fancy Eviction Fails, arXiv 2609.28870](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28870) | SSD tier mostly unnecessary, except with reuse with a long tail | medium | new |
| 2026-09 | [py-kvcache, arXiv 2609.11744](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.11744) | NVMe offload with preload; pays off only depending on setup | medium | new |
| 2026-08 | [Tail-Replay, arXiv 2608.30310](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30310) | Hybrid prefix cache without DeltaNet checkpoints: cache only KV, reconstruct state by replaying the last 5–10 %; 92.8–99.9 % of the quality, TTFT 9.1–14.3× at 32K (5 auth.) | medium: lossy; here an exact checkpoint costs only 56–112 MiB RAM | new |
| 2026-08 | [DASC, arXiv 2608.30386](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30386) | Compress state checkpoints by decay of the heads, 2.63× smaller (Kimi-Linear), TTFT −42.6 %; GDN (Qwen) similar (7 auth.) | medium: only needed when many checkpoints fill the RAM | new |
| 2026-06 | [CacheWise, arXiv 2606.16824](https://huggingface.co/papers/2606.16824) | Coding agents: eviction by tool-call metadata, 2–2.6× fewer evictions | **high** | new |
| 2026-05 | [LMCache on MI300X with Claude Code traces](https://blog.lmcache.ai/en/benchmarking-lmcache-for-multi-turn-agentic-workloads-on-amd-mi300x/) (blog) | 739 Claude Code traces; HBM cache suffices at low load | medium | new |
| 2026-04 | [Sparse Prefix Caching, arXiv 2605.05219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05219) | Exact recurrent states only at a few positions, recompute the rest from the deepest checkpoint; placement as exact DP O(NM), beats block caching with fewer checkpoints (2 auth.) | **high**: exact, fits agent turns with a growing prefix | new |
| NeurIPS 2025 | [KVFlow](https://papers.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/b7971d31a7d5eb0f1eed2f8f6f368195-Abstract-Conference.html) | Eviction by workflow plan instead of LRU, up to 1.8–2.2× | high | new |

## Quantization

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09 | [STEPQuant, arXiv 2609.38169](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38169) | Quantize the delta-rule state: nominal 6 bit ≈ FP32 state, 4 bit better than uniform INT8; sensitive are key rows and heads with a long half-life; measured on Qwen3.8-27B and Kimi-Linear-48B (9 auth.) | **high**: the state (108 MiB F32) is read and written per token; the paper does not examine BF16/FP16, however | new |
| 2026-09 | [Why GDN Survives 4-Bit, arXiv 2609.04098](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04098) | NVFP4 W4A4 on all 496 linear layers of a 27B hybrid incl. GDN gates: −0.52 in the 5-task mean, prefill +14–19 %, FP8 KV with calibrated scales (2 auth.) | medium: NVFP4 does not exist on gfx1200, but "GDN projections tolerate 4 bit" is transferable (≈ 2 B of our dense parameters) | new |
| 2026-09 (v4) | [DynaExq, arXiv 2511.15015](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15015) (v1 2025-11) | Expert precision at runtime: frequent experts high, rare ones low, upgrade and downgrade asynchronously; Qwen3-80B 73.09 → 77.57 % vs. static PTQ at the same device memory, 2.73× at batch 32 (6 auth.) | **high**: fits the expert cache (VRAM share higher, RAM remainder quantized lower) | new |
| 2026-07 | [Proteus, ICML 2026](https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/poster/63948) (poster, no arXiv found) | 2-bit trellis code without lookup tables (computing decoder), better than QTIP-like baselines; decode reads 740 instead of 1,020 GB/s at 70B | medium: 2-bit experts without LUT pressure in LDS; only dense Llama 2 tested | new |
| 2026-04 | [vLLM: FP8 KV and FP8 attention](https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-04-22-fp8-kvcache) (blog, 7 auth.) | e4m3, scale per tensor uncalibrated: NIAH 128K 89 % vs. 91 % BF16, but only after fixing the FP8 accumulation (two-stage in FP32, before 13 %); Qwen3.5-27B MRCR at 1M like baseline; at head_dim 256 TTFT 1.6× | medium: QSA has head_dim 256; lesson: accumulate softmax·V over long contexts in FP32 | new |

## KV quantization

Agent research 2026-10-23, **papers not read**: numbers from abstracts, HTML versions,
blogs, READMEs and PRs (authors' claims). Bytes per token refer to the respective
source, not to our model. The vLLM FP8 blog 2026-04 is above under "Quantization".

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09 | [LMSYS: NVFP4 KV in SGLang](https://www.lmsys.org/blog/2026-09-16-nvfp4-kv-cache) (blog, 2026-09-16) | E2M1 + E4M3 scale per 16 values, global scale 1.0 uncalibrated, no rotation. Qwen3.8-27B against FP8 KV: **SWE-bench Verified 77.80 → 76.20 %** (n = 500), GPQA −1.01 pp., GSM8K −0.31, AIME25 equal; Qwen3.5-397B practically equal. 56 % of the FP8 bytes | **high**: the only coding-agent benchmark on our model family; 4 bit without rotation costs ~1.6 pp. there (just within ~2 SE) | new |
| 2026-09 | [vllm-qwen38-flash-next-kv-quant](https://github.com/andreasgru/vllm-qwen38-flash-next-kv-quant) (GitHub, state 2026-09-02; upstream PR vllm#54846 open) | **Our model**: K/V of the 12 QSA layers in fp8_e4m3 (scale 1.0, 0 % saturation) or nvfp4; **indexer caches stay bf16**. fp8: perplexity +0.12/−0.06/−0.03 % (CIs include 0), NIAH 65K–235K 70/70; nvfp4: **+0.37 to +0.59 %** (outside the CIs), NIAH 70/70, aggregation at ~1M 6/12 unexplained. KV 25.45 / 13.48 / 8.32 KiB/token (bf16/fp8/nvfp4). fp8 decode −0.8 to −1.6 %, nvfp4 prefill 1.75–2.15× slower | **high**: direct measurement on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next; 8 bit unremarkable, 4 bit measurable but small. One machine (SM120), no coding benchmarks | new |
| 2026-09 | [DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, arXiv 2609.19969](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19969) + [FlashMLA README](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/FlashMLA/blob/main/README.md) | FP4 KV plus cross-layer KV reuse: **890 B/token** in HBM, ~1/4 of V4-Flash. FlashMLA formats V4.1: FP8 528 B/token (512 × e4m3 incl. RoPE dims, e8m0 scale per 32), FP4 288 B (e2m1, e4m3 scale per 16); bf16 KV not supported in decode | medium: FP4 KV is co-trained there, not quantized afterwards | new |
| 2026-09 | [Self-Indexing Attention, arXiv 2609.13205](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13205) | Signs of the transformed keys as a 1-bit index for top-k, magnitudes go to any KV compressor (TurboQuant, DSA indexer of V4-Flash tested); 5 % density close to dense on LongBench/RULER, decode attention op up to 10.3× (7 auth.) | low: the only indication that the top-k ranking survives coarse quantization of the index keys | new |
| 2026-09 | [D-Quant, arXiv 2609.19880](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19880) | Entropy coding after rotation into bit streams of fixed length ("drift") so that kernels read regularly; baselines KIVI, QuaRot, TurboQuant, OScaR on RULER 4K–128K (4 auth.) | low: below 4 bit only relevant if capacity does not suffice otherwise | new |
| 2026-08 | [AATC, arXiv 2608.14191](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14191) | Transform coding with bit allocation by reverse water-filling on calibration data: nearly lossless at ~5.8× compression; Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, LongBench/RULER/MATH-500 (5 auth.) | low: calibration per model, own kernel | new |
| 2026-06 | [DeepSeek-V4, arXiv 2606.19348](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19348) | KV: RoPE dims BF16, rest FP8 (~half the size of BF16). Indexer: **QK path in FP4 (MXFP4) trained with QAT**, "cached, loaded and multiplied entirely in FP4"; index scores FP32 → BF16: top-k 2× faster, **recall 99.7 %** | **high** for the indexer: low indexer precision so far exists only with QAT. Whether Qwen's indexer is trained like this: unknown | new |
| 2026-06 | [UltraQuant, arXiv 2606.20474](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20474) (EMNLP 2026 Industry) | FP4 KV with UE8M0 scale per 32 values (4.25 bit), **Hadamard only on K**, QJL removed, on MI355X. Llama-3.1-8B NIAH 99.5 → 100, LongBench-E 54.19 → 53.97; Qwen3.5-35B-A3B RULER 99.38 = 99.38; MiniMax-M2.5 RULER 74.03 → 67.08; **SWE-bench Lite** MiniMax 62 → 59 (FP8 62), Qwen3-235B 28 → 24 (FP8 21). Claude Code trace replay 2.71–4.38× BF16 throughput (7 auth.) | **high**: agent workload; shows the model dependence of 4-bit KV and that K-only rotation suffices | new |
| 2026-06 | [KVarN, arXiv 2606.03458](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03458) | Errors grow in decode over the steps, main cause wrong token scales; Hadamard + variance normalization over both axes, 2 bit, SOTA on MATH500/AIME24/HumanEval according to the abstract (6 auth.) | medium: warning for long generations; prefill-like measurements (PPL) underestimate the damage | new |
| 2026-06 | [RaBitQCache, arXiv 2606.31519](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31519) (ICML 2026) | Rotated binary quantization as a proxy for attention weights, adaptive top-p selection; RULER 79.65 → 79.55 (Llama-3.1-8B) | low: we have the trained indexer | new |
| 2026-05 | [vLLM: TurboQuant study](https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-05-11-turboquant) (blog, 2026-05-11) + [vllm#38479](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/38479) (merged 04-15, v0.20.0), [#39931](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/39931) (Qwen3.5/Qwen3-Next) | Qwen3-30B-A3B MRCR-AUC: BF16 45.8, FP8 43.1, k8v4 43.0, 4bit-nc 42.3, 3bit-nc 31.2. Reasoning: FP8/k8v4 > 98 %, 4bit-nc ~96 %, 3 bit −20 pp. Throughput 73–80 % of BF16 (Triton). Conclusion: **"FP8 remains the best default"**, k8v4 without advantage over FP8 | **high**: independent measurement, 8 bit is the consensus, 3 bit out | new |
| 2026-05 | [Hurwitz-Quaternion-MQ, arXiv 2605.27646](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27646) | 4D lattice VQ; 4.89 bit close to fp16 on RULER subtasks (Qwen3-8B, 4K/8K), HotpotQA weaker at 8K | low | new |
| 2026-04 | [SAW-INT4, arXiv 2604.19157](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19157) | INT4 per token and head (scale + zero point over head_dim) with block-diagonal Hadamard-128 **only on K**. **Qwen3-4B/8B naive INT4 = 0 on all 5 tasks**, with rotation 73.78 vs. 75.64 and 68.15 (K+V rotated 69.97) vs. 70.84 BF16 (GPQA, HumanEval, LCB v6, AIME25, MATH500); GLM-4.7 already nearly lossless naively. VQ and Hessian bring little; fused rotation without measurable cost (11 auth.) | **high**: Qwen keys have hard outliers, 4-bit K only with rotation; recipe simple enough for an own kernel | new |

Qwen3-8B q4_0 7.645 → 7.501 (F16 7.320), q8_0 unchanged; **Qwen3.5-9B KLD q8_0 0.00087 → 0.00080, q4_0 0.0080 → 0.0048**; gpt-oss-20b AIME25 q4_0 2.0 → 21.7 %, q8_0 31.7 → 37.1 % (F16 37.9). Qwen3.5-4B (head_dim 256) q4_0 already without rotation 8.348 vs. 8.327. tg 0.88–0.93×. TurboQuant types rejected upstream ([#21089](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/21089) closed 2026-06-02) | **high**: q8_0 is practically lossless; Qwen3.5 hybrids (gated attention, head_dim 256) tolerate KV quantization much better than Qwen3 | new |

Recommendation fp8_e4m3 | medium: FP4 without rotation collapses on hard tasks, simple benchmarks hide it | new |

Indexer cache (128 per token) separate, per page of 64 first values, then scales (dtype not named in the blog). Hadamard removed because without measurable effect | medium: template for the indexer layout; keeping RoPE dims high-precision is standard there | new |
| 2025-06, ICML 2025 | [CommVQ, arXiv 2506.18879](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18879) | Additive VQ with RoPE-commutative codebook (EM-trained), decoding folded into the attention; 2 bit = −87.5 %, 1 bit with little loss, Llama-3.1-8B with 128K on one RTX 4090 (11 auth.) | medium: strongest compression, but codebook training per model and an own kernel; unchecked with partial RoPE (64 of 256) | new |
| 2025-05, ICLR 2026 | [PM-KVQ, arXiv 2505.18610](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18610) | Long CoT: quantization errors accumulate per step; progressive bit reduction and block-wise mixed precision | medium: argument for "new tokens high precision, old ones lower" | new |
| 2025-05 | [Gated Attention, arXiv 2505.06708](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06708) | Gate after SDPA, "attention-sink-free": mitigates attention sinks, better context extrapolation (13 auth.) | medium: QSA uses this gate; plausible explanation for the robustness of the Qwen3.5/3.8 KV (hypothesis) | new |
| 2025-04 | [TurboQuant, arXiv 2504.19874](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19874) | Random rotation + optimal scalar quantizer per coordinate + 1-bit QJL on the remainder (unbiased inner product); KV quality-neutral at 3.5 bit/channel, slightly worse at 2.5 (4 auth.) | medium: theory anchor; production measurements (vLLM, UltraQuant) show that QJL and 3 bit do not hold up | new |
| 2025-02 | [PolarQuant, arXiv 2502.02617](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02617) | Preconditioning, then polar coordinates; angles need no scale/zero point; > 4.2× compression (5 auth.) | low | new |
| 2025-02, ICML 2025 | [KVTuner, arXiv 2502.04420](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04420) | Precision per layer for K and V separately; **K more sensitive than V**; Llama-3.1-8B nearly lossless at 3.25 bit, Qwen2.5-7B needs 4.0 bit (9 auth.) | medium: supports K8V4 as a first step below 8 bit | new |
| 2025-01 | [RotateKV, arXiv 2501.16383](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16383) | Outlier-aware Hadamard rotation with channel reordering, rotation before RoPE grouped over heads, protection of attention sinks; 2 bit < 0.3 PPL (Llama-2-13B), GSM8K < 1.7 % (7 auth.) | low | new |

KVQuant: K per channel **before RoPE**, non-uniform levels, ~1 % outliers stored sparsely, 3 bit. QServe: KV4 with SmoothAttention. QJL: 1-bit JL signs for K without scales | medium: terms and baselines; K per channel fits paged layout and streaming poorly, replaced 2025/26 by rotation + per token | new |

## Open gaps (state of the second search 2026-10-22)

The four gaps of the first search are worked off. What is still missing:

- **DeltaNet state BF16 vs. FP16 vs. F32** at B=1 and long generations: no paper measures this
  directly. ReplaySSM: FP16 close to F32 only with stochastic rounding; STEPQuant compares only with F32;
  FlashInfer uses BF16 without a published measurement. Measure ourselves (open question 1 in 06).
- **Quantized indexer keys** (open question 4): nothing found.
- **FP8 activations for MoE prefill**: no reliable measurement, W8A8 vs. W4A8 on WMMA open.
- **RDNA3/4 in papers**: no arXiv work measures on consumer RDNA; only blogs, issues, repos.
- [AgentSpec, arXiv 2608.24004](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24004) (speculation for agent workflows) names no numbers in the
  abstract; check the full text before it goes into the table.

## 2026-10-26: Thinking loops under greedy and speculative sampling (two agent research runs)

Occasion: with greedy, the thinking ran into literal loops in 6 of 8 hard tasks (measurements/2026-10-26-effort).
[V] full text/page read, [S] search hits only.

| Date | Source | Core | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-12 | [V] arXiv 2512.12895 "Wait, Wait, Wait… Why Do Reasoning Models Loop?" | Loops decrease with temperature, more frequent on hard tasks and distilled models; confidence in the repetition grows each round | **high**: explains our finding | sampling implemented as the default |
| 2026-08 | [V] arXiv 2608.22761 DRY sampler | Penalty only for tokens that extend a seen sequence; loop rate roughly halved, benchmarks close to base; in llama.cpp (PR #9702, merged) | medium: option for greedy | open |
| 2026-01 | [S] arXiv 2601.05693 "Circular Reasoning" | Entropy collapses at the start of a loop; parameters alone do not solve it | medium: cheap signal for a loop guard | open |
| 2025-11 | [S] arXiv 2511.00536 WordSaladChopper | Classifier on hidden states detects repetition, cuts off | low: training needed | – |
| 2025-12 | [V] arXiv 2512.04419 | Beam search best_of 5 eliminates repetition, presence penalty helps only partly | low: expensive, poor with drafts | – |
| 2023 | Leviathan et al. arXiv 2211.17192 | Speculative sampling: draft from q, acceptance min(1, p/q), residual max(0, p − q); acceptance Σ min(p, q) | **high** | implemented: decode with sampling 55.5 → 64.5 tok/s |
| 2026-02 | [S] arXiv 2602.23881 (LK Losses), EAGLE-3 | Acceptance length T 0 3.75, T 1 3.39 (−10 %) | Yardstick: sampling should cost hardly any hits | here: ~3 greedy, 2.9 sampling |
| 2024/25 | [S] Sequoia 2402.12374, Multi-Draft 2502.18779, Cactus 2604.04987 | Tree/multi-drafts for sampling, Cactus relaxes fidelity in a controlled way | medium: next lever for sampling | open |

No engine (llama.cpp, vLLM, SGLang, Ollama, LM Studio) has built-in loop detection; llama.cpp has
`--reasoning-budget` as a hard cap ([S] issues #25067, #20429).

## 2026-10-26: Nanbeige4.3-33B (agent research, HF card and config.json read)

Nanbeige4.3-33B (HF 2026-10-24, Apache-2.0): **dense**, Llama layout (52 layers, hidden 7168, GQA 56/8),
vocabulary 166,912, context 512K, no MTP on the card. Vendor numbers: Terminal-Bench 2.1 80.9, DeepSWE 65.5,
SWE-Bench Pro 60.3 (Qwen3.8-27B 61.7); not independently confirmed. Not worthwhile for us: dense ~19 GB per token
(ceiling ~16 passes/s against ~62 for Flash-Next), different tokenizer (no drafter), too large as a worker. To watch:
independent numbers and the announced small 4.3 models (distillation from the 33B). Relevance low / new.

Little new that is directly usable; most hits confirm existing findings. arXiv API was rate-limited, so speculative-decoding, sparse-attention and low-bit searches are incomplete. GitHub (`gh search`) gave no results, nothing reported there.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-29 | [PDScope, arXiv 2610.39216](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39216) (full text read, 9 auth., PPoPP'27) | Hardware: A100 + 2x Xeon Gold 6230R, and RTX 3090 + EPYC 7452 (16 cores), both PCIe 4.0 x16; BF16 Mixtral-8x7B, Qwen3-30B-A3B, DeepSeek-V2-Lite, Moonlight-16B (V4-Flash INT8 only for scaling). Baselines: Fate, KTransformers v0.3.2, Fiddler, HybriMoE, MoE-APEX; prefill up to 10.3x Fate, 8.6x KTransformers, 2.5x MoE-APEX, decode up to 6.1x Fate and 2.9x KTransformers/Fiddler, 2.1x HybriMoE/MoE-APEX; single-batch DeepSeek-V4-Flash decode only 1.26-1.97x. Mechanism: "aggressive" (prefill) = a cost model (Eq. 3-4) sorts the experts of the current and the predicted next layer by pending tokens and moves every expert to the GPU whose PCIe copy + GPU time beats CPU execution, plus prefetching next-layer experts into the CPU idle bubble; "conservative" (decode) = four modes around the balance point n_c = argmin max((k-n_c)*t_g, n_c*t_c): load on demand only, prefetch only, unload only, or cross-layer, and prefetch only if the expected utility R_hit*gain - (1-R_hit)*loss > 0 (single-layer lookahead). Parts: learned per-layer predictor (trained on 128 ShareGPT batches, online fine-tune at 25 % error), static per-layer graph recompiled once the true gate is known, pre-registered pinned buffers (ping-pong + prefetch), 3 CUDA streams per expert, 66-80 % PCIe peak. Ablation: AsyncIO 1.67-1.88x prefill / 1.34-2.24x decode, predictor+scheduler 1.39-2.06x prefill / 1.17-1.38x decode; hot-expert table off: -1.8 % prefill, -8.2 % decode. Versus us: all baselines are BF16 on a CPU that runs experts; our experts are IQ1/IQ2 (2 MB, ~36 us over PCIe), CPU experts and predictors already measured as no gain in prefill (18-moe-prefill-streaming.md section 5), layer-wise prefill and cache exist. The only unfamiliar piece is the expected-utility prefetch rule (gain at hit rate vs. wasted PCIe time), which is close to `IE_PRE_KEEP`/confidence-gated preload in 14-expert-prefetch.md. **Verdict: no ticket** (comparison with our code only via the notes, not re-measured) | **medium** | new, read |

IE_SPEC_CUT result) | new |
| 2026-10-25 | [Speculative Decoding for MoE: Expert Bytes, Kernel Bandwidth and the Best Draft Length, arXiv 2610.34440](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34440) (abstract, 1 auth.) | Step time = bytes / effective bandwidth + fixed; best draft length is a staircase in acceptance rate; deduplicating expert reads across draft slots pays off only at ~5-8 tokens per pass or never (per-slot kernel has higher bandwidth, r = 0.32-0.75); calibration from one layer's expert op | medium-high: gives a cheap model to set draft length; Metal/CUDA only | new |
| 2026-10-24 | [COPE, arXiv 2610.33404](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33404) (abstract, 2 auth.) | Emulator picks the best MoE offloading policy from profiled operator schedules plus offline routing traces; separates expert residency from execution strategy; 4 new policies, ranking accuracy +24-100 % | medium: matches our trace-replay approach for cache policy | new |

Observation: 84 % of expert routes in short prefills go to a stable set that fits in VRAM, so those experts run on the GPU in every forward pass, the rest of the routes split so under one of six routed experts per layer per token stays on the CPU; long prefills upload each distinct cold expert once; decode is one fixed-shape graph per GPU. Median wait after a tool output -22 to -55 %, sessions 1.4-2.5x faster than llama.cpp/KTransformers | **high**: the agent loop (short prefill after each tool output) is our main workload; tests whether a "resident set for short prefill" beats our current split of prefill modes | new |

## 2026-11-11 (week 46): gap closure 2026-10-24 to 2026-11-10 (tool scan, complete pagination)

`tools/papers/scan.py` (topics v1), arXiv by submission date, 7-day windows, every query paginated to `totalResults`
([coverage book](papers-coverage.md)): 575 hits, 517 not yet in this file. Read: all titles; abstracts of about 70 hits
(the API abstract is the text of the abstract page; no full texts). Entries already listed above are not repeated.
All entries are arXiv preprints; numbers are the authors', nothing was measured here. "abstract" = abstract page only; status `read (full text)` = HTML full text read, with evaluation hardware, models, batch size and code link given in the status cell. Rows without a status of this kind are abstract-only.

| 2026-11-01 | [Apto, arXiv 2611.01859](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01859) (abstract, 3 auth.) | CPU/GPU hybrid MoE on **shared PCs**: CPU contention from colocated applications unbalances a fixed CPU/GPU split. CPU and GPU executors claim experts from opposite ends of a dual-ended queue, so the split adapts without predicting contention; affinity-aware scheduling, cancellable tail racing for the last expert. Prefill 2.96x and decode 1.52x standalone, 5.97x and 3.45x under concurrent CPU load; coding-agent applications 2.02x on average | **high**: same scenario as our work-beside-the-desktop priority (static split breaks under load); the dual-ended queue is a cheap elastic scheduler for our CPU-expert path | read (full text): RTX 5080 16 GB, Core Ultra 9 285K, 96 GB DRAM, PCIe 5.0 x16; Qwen1.5-MoE, DeepSeek-V2-Lite, Qwen3-30B-A3B (no quantization stated, no DeltaNet), batch 1 and 4, baselines FineMoE, llama.cpp, KTransformers, HybriMoE; agent replay 2.02x (range 1.04-4.07x); no code; on unified memory only 81-88 % of llama.cpp; authors' numbers |

OLMoE-1B-7B, 50 %/25 % resident on RTX 4090: 2.2-2.3x vs. exact routing with LRU, 25-30 % over Cache-Prior, benchmark differences +0.1/+0.5 points | medium: lossy routing change, but the determinism argument fits `TONA_DETERMINISTIC`; we found prefetch gains small (IE_PRE_KEEP) | new |

Nine repositories: llama.cpp, vLLM, SGLang, MLX, mlx-lm, ggml and, because `ROCm/ROCm` is renamed to `legacy-rocm-build`
and cannot be searched, `ROCm/rocm-systems`, `ROCm/TheRock`, `ROCm/rocm-libraries`. 9397 PRs (SGLang 3190, vLLM 2967,
rocm-systems 1134, rocm-libraries 991, llama.cpp 672, TheRock 319, MLX 79, mlx-lm 35, ggml 10) and 1120 issues (by
topic term and repository) found; every one has a row in [papers-screened.tsv](papers-screened.tsv). Screening depth:
titles of **all** llama.cpp, MLX, mlx-lm and ggml PRs and issues; for vLLM and SGLang only titles that match a
keyword filter (expert cache/offload, DeltaNet/GDN, Qwen3-Next/3.8, DFlash, hybrid cache, RDNA/gfx12, unified memory,
IQ1, state replay/rollback/checkpoint, YaRN; 301 titles read, matches without a read PR body are `note`), the rest
`drop` as `bulk-no-keyword`; ROCm repository PRs `drop` as `rocm-internal` (CI and library internals, gfx12 hits not
read). PR bodies were read for the entries below (marked "body read"). Numbers are the PR authors', from their text;
"merged" is the state on 2026-11-11 (checked with `gh`). Open ones are on [papers-watch.tsv](papers-watch.tsv).

### Gap closure 2026-11-11 (tool scan, complete pagination, added 2026-11-12)

`scan.py arxiv --from 2026-11-11 --to 2026-11-11`: 0 hits in all 11 topic queries, `complete=True`. This is the API's state, not a quiet day: when the scan ran (2026-11-12 04:47 UTC) the newest `cs.LG` entry of the whole API was dated 2026-11-10 18:58 UTC and a plain `submittedDate` query for 2026-11-11 returned `totalResults` 0 (for 2026-11-10: 1136); the index lags. The day must be searched again once arXiv lists it (see the re-run note at the end of this section). `scan.py github` (PRs and issues): 635 PRs (SGLang 250, vLLM 170, rocm-systems 78, rocm-libraries 58, llama.cpp 49, TheRock 26, MLX 4) and 76 issues; all 711 have a row in the screened book. Depth: titles of **all** llama.cpp, ggml, MLX and mlx-lm items read; vLLM, SGLang and ROCm only the titles that match the keyword filter (614 rows `keyword-filter`); bodies of the PRs below read in full where marked, otherwise the first 500 characters (`abstract-read`). Numbers are the authors'.

| Date | PR / issue | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-11 | [llama.cpp #32082](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/32082) (open, full body read, author numbers, no review yet), sister PoC [#32114](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/32114) (FFN-only chunking), prototype [#32066](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/32066) (closed, full body read) | **Layer-first prefill** for MoE with experts in RAM (`-cmoe` with the `llama_moe_cache`): the loop order changes from "per ubatch, all layers" to "per layer, all chunks of N tokens", QKV, norms, RoPE, KV store, attention and the expert FFN all chunked (`LLAMA_LAYER_MAJOR=<chunk>`), so each layer's experts cross PCIe once per N tokens. Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-class model (48 layers, 128 GB RAM, 32 GB VRAM): warm prefill 1275 (master, ub 4096) -> 1937 (N 8192) -> 2802 (N 16384) -> 3516 t/s (N 32768), decode 43.2 -> 41.4 -> 39.6 t/s because the larger compute buffer takes cache VRAM (hit rate 82 -> 75 %); gemma-4-26B-A4B prefill 3610-3640 -> 7840-7900 t/s (2.17x, N 16384, chunk 2048). Same author's earlier prototype #32066 (upload layer l+1 while l computes): prefill +15 % but decode -9 % because its 1950 MiB buffer comes out of the cache, net loss for a 21 % prefill / 79 % decode mix; the layer-major idea was named there as the next step ("could be as fast as 3500 t/s") | **high**: it is the technique we have as layer-wise prefill (arXiv 2510.08055, bit-identical, +7-11 % at 13K), now with an independent number and the decode price (compute buffer takes cache VRAM) stated; compare chunk size N against our VRAM split | new, watched |
| 2026-11-11 | [llama.cpp #32076](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/32076) (open, body read, two comments) | `llama_moe_cache` without host-device syncs, `GGML_OP_CUSTOM` carries the signalling between host and GPU; "huge speed-up expected on Vulkan and Windows", about 10 % on CUDA/Linux; AI use disclosed | medium: the sync cost of the CPU-GPU handshake is the problem our megakernel handshake (yield after 100 ms) also has; the design is op-graph based, ours is in-kernel waiting | new, watched |
| 2026-11-11 | [llama.cpp #32081](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/32081) (open draft, full body read) | `--batch-invariant`: opt-in flag (proc-address function the backends may implement) that pins the kernel configuration for all batch sizes, because a different accumulation order per batch size flips near-tied greedy tokens (speculative verify vs single-token decode, different ubatch sizes); tested with `test-backend-ops` on Metal and CUDA, costs speed; refs issue #25618 | **high** for `TONA_DETERMINISTIC` and speculative verification: the same mechanism we have to control (verify batch against single-token decode must give the same token); no hardware numbers | new, watched |
| 2026-11-11 | [vLLM #67045](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67045), [#67053](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67053), [#67064](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67064); SGLang [#49698](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/49698), [#49886](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/49886), [#49719](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/49719); tracking issue [SGLang #49944](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/49944) | Hybrid (Mamba/GDN) prefix caching, all open (first 500 characters of the bodies): exact Mamba states under speculative decoding (one state column per draft position, a stop can land inside the accepted draft), states retained when decode crosses a block boundary, P/D loads cut at the Mamba checkpoint; SGLang: reuse cached full-attention KV while replaying GDN state (opt-in `SGLANG_ENABLE_GDN_KV_REUSE=1`), checkpoint hybrid state only at fork points and request ends, replay rings in a unified-memory sub-pool | medium: same questions as our recurrent-state checkpoints for the prefix cache; no hardware numbers read | new (abstract) |

## 2026-11-18 (week 47): weekly search, gap closure 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-17 (tool scan, complete pagination; numbers are the authors')

Tool runs (topics v1): `scan.py arxiv --from 2026-11-11 --to 2026-11-15` complete (99 hits, 93 new); `--to 2026-11-17` gave 164 hits but is marked incomplete by the tool because arXiv needs 3 days to list a date, so **2026-11-16 and 2026-11-17 are still open in the coverage ledger** and must be searched again from 2026-11-19 (`scan.py coverage` lists them). `scan.py github` 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-17: 3198 PRs (SGLang 1015, vLLM 987, rocm-systems 401, rocm-libraries 381, llama.cpp 264, TheRock 103, MLX 25, mlx-lm 13, ggml 9) and 526 issues, complete, 617 requests. All 164 + 3724 hits have a row in the screened book. Depth: every title of llama.cpp, ggml, MLX and mlx-lm read (`title-read`); vLLM, SGLang and the ROCm repositories only the titles that match a keyword filter (gfx12, RDNA, DFlash, GDN, Qwen3.8, offload, megakernel, ...: 235 titles read), the rest `keyword-filter`; all 164 arXiv titles read, abstracts of about 45 read. `scan.py watch` re-run (changes below). ### Models (Hugging Face API, 2026-11-18)

|---|---|
| Qwen4 | **No official Qwen4 release.** The Qwen organisation's newest repositories are Qwen3.8 (Flash-Next, 27B, 2.4T-A95B, last edits 2026-11-07: chat-template and generation tags only). Third-party repositories named "Qwen4-27B/32B" (abliterated, uncensored, GGUF; 2026-11-12 to 11-17) carry no base-model information or point at `Qwen/Qwen4-Flash-Next` (does not resolve); one GGUF reports architecture `qwen35`, 262144 context. Read as relabelled Qwen3.8 derivatives, unverified. ### arXiv 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-15 (complete) and 11-16 to 11-17 (incomplete)

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-15 | [CECAR, arXiv 2611.20119](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20119) (abstract, 7 auth.) | MoE with experts fetched from SSD: cache hit rate is bounded even under Belady, binary cache-aware routing is unstable; an ML policy predicts each expert's reuse distance and routing prefers experts with short predicted distance. Qwen3-30B-A3B, 16 GB consumer GPU, experts on SSD: 12.5 tok/s, 4.5x over conventional MoE inference, above baselines with the whole model in DRAM; [code](https://github.com/hoonki2002/CECAR) | **high** (ticket): same setting as our expert streaming (16 GB VRAM, SSD/RAM tier); changes the *routing*, i.e. alters the model output, so it falls under rule 3 (quality check) and "lossy only after a quality decision"; compare the reuse-distance predictor with our cache policy | new (abstract) |
| 2026-11-16 | [DNeo, arXiv 2611.21925](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21925) (abstract, 8 auth.) | Parallel (DFlash-style) drafter with better feature encoding: Mixture-of-Features Attention, a working memory of rejected proposals, 2-tap causal convolutions, prefix-only cross-attention, non-uniform target layer selection; 1.3x faster generation than DFlash-2 at equal drafter size and quality | medium-high: DFlash successor; needs a trained drafter for our target, so it is a candidate for the drafter conversation, not for the engine | new (abstract) |
| 2026-11-14 | [Text Speeding Itself Up, arXiv 2611.19455](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19455) | Training-free session-local drafter: recency-weighted, backing-off, confidence-gated session memory beats prompt-lookup decoding (copy from the most recent match instead of the earliest); paired bootstrap intervals; general/medical/legal text, no code shown in the abstract | medium: costs almost nothing per step, useful beside MTP/DFlash for repetitive code; check on our coding traces | new (abstract) |
| 2026-11-17 | [SpecSearch, arXiv 2611.22296](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22296) (3 auth.) | Hardware-aware search of decoding strategy, draft length and precision; on an RTX 5090 DFlash gives 3.50x over autoregressive at low concurrency but 0.76x at high concurrency with short outputs; average 2.01x over AR and 1.36x over speculative decoding with vLLM on 5090, H100, L40, MI300X | medium: confirms that speculation pays only while memory-bound (batch 1 is our case); draft length per hardware is something to tune on gfx1200 | new (abstract) |
| 2026-11-16 | [Lost in a Few Heads, arXiv 2611.21917](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21917) (2 auth.) | Sparse attention loses long-context retrieval because the block selector under-ranks the needle in a few blind heads (mean-pooled key summary dilutes one decisive token); full attention in those heads or a 4-bit scorer keeps the budget and recovers 54.1 -> 97.5 % on MiniCPM4.1-8B at 64K; same few heads in NSA, SeerAttention-R, DashAttention; 84-89 % of the TTFT saving kept | medium: our model's QSA indexer is a block/top-k selector too; a per-head calibration for the indexer is a possible long-context (1M) quality lever; checkpoints are not Qwen | new (abstract) |

`--to 2026-11-18` and `--to 2026-11-19` are marked incomplete by the tool (the API lists a date only after 3 days), so **arXiv 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19 stay open** in the
ledger and are searched again from 2026-11-21 (11-18) and 2026-11-22 (11-19). Their 68 hits seen so far were screened already (title or abstract) so they are not read twice.

[#62480](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62480) (W4A8 MoE decode kernel for gfx11) merged; nothing else changed state.

| 2026-11-18 | [Twindex, arXiv 2611.25078](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25078) (abstract, 6 auth.) | With main KV offloaded to host, the indexer cache is the HBM bottleneck for sparse-attention models (63 % of per-request HBM on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next at 1M tokens); keep a compact copy in HBM (shared component + compressed residual, learned offline), pick a larger candidate set from it, let the model's own indexer pick top-k from the full rows in host memory; 1.62-3.03x more requests at 1M | medium-high: our QSA indexer pool is 436 MB per slot at 256K and about 1.7 GB at 1M in VRAM (README); a compact pool in VRAM plus full rows in RAM would give back slots at long context; needs the offline-learned copy, check cost | new (abstract) |

there; numbers are the authors'. The entries that matter most for the Mac backend:

| 2026-10 | [DeepInfer-Mac, arXiv 2610.36564](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36564) (full text) | 748B MoE on a MacBook Air M4 24 GB: one 16 KiB-padded record per expert read uncached into reusable GPU buffers; next-layer prefetch +21 % | high | new |

## Backfill 2026-10 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-23 as part of the backfill 2025-01-01 to 2026-10-23, newest month first. Tool and screening scheme as in the 2026-11-11 section; deviations: the arXiv and GitHub title filters named below (reading all 790 + 12600 titles does not fit), and method per row in the screened book: `title-filter` (arXiv) and `bulk-no-keyword` (GitHub) mean the title was NOT read (keyword filter only), every other row was read by title; CLOSED 2026-11-12: all 634 arXiv titles and all 1158 llama.cpp, ggml, MLX and mlx-lm titles of this month were read afterwards (section "Title read-through 2026-10" at the end of this backfill; the book column `method` says per row how deep). vLLM, SGLang and the ROCm repositories stay keyword-filtered by design. The GitHub drops live in a local file (`work/papers/screened-github-drops.tsv`), the coverage book remains the proof of completeness.

### arXiv 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-23

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 790 hits, 776 not in this file before. Screening
depth: a title filter (about 20 topic words in the title, an efficiency word in the abstract; exclusions for diffusion,
vision, RL, training, other modalities) selected 142 titles, all of them read, abstracts for about 60; the other 634 are
`drop` as `title-filter` in the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). Abstract-only unless noted; numbers are the authors'.
Status `read (full text)`: HTML full text read for evaluation hardware and code.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-21 | [PACE, arXiv 2610.29545](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29545) (3 auth.) | Four-bit **gated-delta state** quantizer: round the state only once every few tokens (like chunked kernels do per chunk), keep the tokens in between in a small buffer; large state entries sit in columns, so one scale per column; the heads that keep rounding error longest stay at 8 bit. Four hybrid models 4B-35B: mixed 4/8-bit competitive with 8-bit DAMP on the three larger ones at about two thirds of the state size; periodic INT4 decoding has lower TPOT than per-token bf16 from batch 16 | **high**: our DeltaNet state is F32 (108 MiB, `mega_alloc.cpp`) and rewritten every token; this is the recipe for 8/4-bit and for a cheaper update cadence | read (full text): one H100 PCIe, Qwen3.5-4B, 512-token context, batch 1-128; no code link found; large-batch regime, B=1 effect unknown |
| 2026-10-11 | [Preserving Updates in Low-Bit Recurrent State Quantization, arXiv 2610.15709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15709) (4 auth.) | Per-step state rounding errors are coherent and come from small updates lost in rounding; Low-Rank Bypass accumulates small updates in a low-rank buffer and merges the large elements periodically; Bounded Directional Rounding rounds reversed elements toward their update (larger error but better model) | **high**: the failure class a low-bit DeltaNet state would face (ours is F32 today), with a rounding rule that costs no storage | read (full text): batch-size motivation, no hardware line found in the text; code not found |
| 2026-10-10 | [When Dynamic Scaling Fails, arXiv 2610.15139](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15139) (1 auth.) | INT4 recurrent state that recalls perfectly at step 0 destroys it 1000 steps later (scale-state feedback, norm explosion); anchoring the scale to a full-precision reference recovers only 1.8 of 5.4 nats; outlier-aware schemes lose to plain persistent scaling; group-wise INT4 at 32K is worth no more than discarding the state | **high** (warning): certify a state bit width by decode horizon, not by the first tokens | read (full text): single RTX 3090, WikiText-103, public checkpoints; no code link found |
| 2026-10-20 | [BitMTP, arXiv 2610.28500](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28500) (5 auth.; code PolyU-VCLab/BitMTP) | Combining ultra-low-bit backbones with MTP fails naively (masked drafting lacks the intermediate inputs; wider computation and KV updates eat the saving); algorithm-system co-design, hybrid decoding up to about 2x on RTX 3090 and Jetson Orin | medium: low-bit backbone plus MTP is our IQ1_M plus drafter question; consumer GPU, but NVIDIA and needs trained heads | read (full text): H20, RTX 3090, Jetson Orin NX, non-FP8 draft projections on Ampere |
| 2026-10-01 | [Post-Training MTP Heads, arXiv 2610.00888](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00888) (11 auth.) | Frozen Qwen3-8B with K=3 chained MTP heads trained after the fact on about 2.5B target-generated tokens reaches the expected speedup of jointly trained MiMo-7B (10^3-10^4 fewer tokens); chain-aware relaxed verification | medium: cheap drafter heads for a frozen target; relaxed verification is lossy | new |
| 2026-10-01 | [RapidMoE, arXiv 2610.01265](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01265) (5 auth.) | CPU-GPU hybrid MoE: offloading at bit level instead of expert level (residual split: compact decoupled storage, dual-path routing aligned with hardware, balanced execution) against PCIe-bound expert loading and CPU-bound compute | medium: another point in the expert-vs-CPU split space; lossy status not clear from the abstract | new |

| 2026-10-06 | [Quantization Effects on Tool-Failure Recovery, arXiv 2610.07781](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07781) (1 auth.) | 8-bit vs 4-bit Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-7B on 20 deterministic tool tasks, 5 prompts: the direction of the recovery difference flips with prompt and evaluation (0 to +20 points for Llama, -50 to +35 for Qwen) | medium: tool-failure recovery of quantized models is prompt-sensitive; confirms trajectory-level checks | new |
| 2026-10-10 | [TurnCost, arXiv 2610.15093](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15093) (2 auth.) | 600 public coding-agent trajectories: median observation 118-286 tokens, 98.6 % of input tokens served from a prefix cache, turn count explains 84 % of log trajectory cost (observation size 4 %) | medium: the number of turns, not the context size, drives cost; supports prefix cache over compaction | new |

| 2026-10-16 | [Acceptance Is Not Speed, arXiv 2610.23818](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23818) (4 auth.) and [When Does Better Acceptance Pay?, arXiv 2610.24993](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24993) (13 auth.) | Relaxed verification accepts 1.34x tokens per round but makes completions 1.54x longer (length inflation), accuracy drops; under shared training and inference conditions published drafter gaps shrink | medium: argues against lossy acceptance rules, and for measuring wall-clock | new |

### Title read-through 2026-10 (second pass, all remaining titles read)

arXiv: all 634 rows that were `title-filter` were read by title (634 titles, `method` `title-read`); 90 of them got the
abstract (`abstract-read`), nine candidates got the HTML page searched for hardware, batch size and code links (the full
text was not read end to end: `fulltext-read` here means the evaluation setup was extracted from the full text). Result: 9
candidates, 70 notes, the rest dropped. GitHub: all 1158 titles of llama.cpp (781 PRs, 161 issues), ggml (9, 1), mlx
(108, 38) and mlx-lm (52, 8) were read by title: 17 candidates, 94 notes, 1047 drops (`title-read-no-match`); bodies read
with `gh` for the candidates marked. Numbers are the authors'; states are from 2026-11-12. Nothing in this pass is rated
**high** for an arXiv paper: the 634 titles held no new recurrent-state or MoE-offload paper that the first pass had
missed, the finds are on the GitHub side.

| Date | Paper / PR | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Backfill 2026-09 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30, second month of the backfill. Tool and screening scheme as in the 2026-11-11 section; this time **every** title of the four mandatory sources was read (no keyword filter there); the column `method` of the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv) says per row how deep: `title-read`, `abstract-read`, `fulltext-read` (the evaluation setup, code links and batch size were extracted from the HTML page, the text was not read end to end) or `keyword-filter`.

### arXiv 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 955 hits, 943 not in this file before. All 955 titles read; 214 abstracts opened (the first 380 characters, the setup lines of the HTML for 12 papers). Result: 25 candidates for stage 2 (`high` 6; 11 with the HTML setup extracted), 168 notes, the rest dropped. Numbers are the authors'.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-25 | [Low-Bit Recurrent States in Hybrid Language Models, arXiv 2609.30950](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30950) (2 auth.) | Calibration-free mixed-precision bit allocation for the recurrent state from the observability Gramian and the state ranges, log-quantized decay rates; per-token 4-bit mean payload cuts the excess NLL 3.3-27.9x against the best of seven baselines on three hybrid models; at 6 bit within 0.005 nats of an FP32 state; with less frequent write-back the gain shrinks | **high**: our DeltaNet state is F32 and written every token; this gives the bit allocation for 4-6 bit | read (setup extracted, no hardware or code line found; perplexity-type evaluation) |
| 2026-09-29 | [LeapQuant, arXiv 2609.38166](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38166) (13 auth.) | Training-free near-lossless 8-bit state quantization for GDN/KDA: quantize once per window of tokens and compute the in-window outputs from the fixed low-bit state plus a high-precision buffer of updates; keep the largest rows and columns in high precision; end-to-end speedups on B200, RTX PRO 6000 and RTX 5090 | **high**: the same per-window idea as PACE (2610.29545, in the 2026-10 section) from a second group, with consumer-GPU kernels; the window write-back is the cheaper update cadence we need | read (setup extracted): B200, RTX PRO 6000, RTX 5090, batch up to 256, GLM-5.x-class models with GDN/KDA layers; code not found |
| 2026-09-30 | [Low-Discrepancy Dither for Quantized Recurrent State Caches, arXiv 2609.39185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39185) (1 auth.; code nssprogrammer/ssm-dither) | A deterministic golden-ratio Weyl dither beats stochastic rounding (which production systems use) on pure and hybrid models over long decode horizons at no cost; round-to-nearest looks best in short tests and falls far behind later because it discards small updates | **high**: a rounding rule for a low-bit state write-back (ours is F32 today) that is deterministic (fits `TONA_DETERMINISTIC`) and long-horizon safe; with 2610.15139 it argues for checking the state at long decode lengths | read (setup extracted): notebooks, Triton kernel and hybrid-model hooks public; hardware not stated in the extract |
| 2026-09-03 | [When Quantization Breaks Memory: Recurrent-State Write-Back, arXiv 2609.04490](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04490) (3 auth.) | In a small GRU, deterministic 4-bit state storage raises the lifetime-estimation errors about 70x and 300x: updates below the write threshold leave the stored state frozen; error feedback, residual memory and direction memory repair it | **high** (mechanism): the same failure class as the other three, shown in isolation; not an LLM | read (setup extracted): fluorescence-lifetime GRU, not a language model |
| 2026-09-27 | [SuffixReplay, arXiv 2609.33477](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33477) (9 auth.) | Prefix caching for hybrid LLMs without recurrent-state checkpoints: linear states forget the distant past, so the state at a page boundary is approximated by replaying only a recent suffix; reuse at every cache-supported page boundary | **high**: our hybrid prefix cache keeps full checkpoints; this trades them for replay compute with a decay argument (lossy: needs a quality check on our model) | read (setup extracted): vLLM issues and PR cited; hardware line not found |
| 2026-09-20 | [LumoTree, arXiv 2609.23900](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23900) (1 auth.; artifact MaCoredroid/Lumo_FlyWheel) | Tree speculative verification for recurrent-hybrid models: independent paths verified in parallel, recurrent state tiles reused, boundary states between dependent paths, accepted-path replay into the running state, convolution gathering and attention-cache remapping; MTP drafter state and batch above one are outside the comparison | **high**: the state-rollback problem of tree verification on GDN layers, with an open artifact | read (setup extracted): single-author, artifact public; GPU not named in the extract |
| 2026-09-16 | [Edge0: Serving 35B MoEs from SSD, arXiv 2609.18063](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18063) (5 auth.; code Edge0-AI/Edge0) | A per-layer head predicts the next layer's routing one token ahead and that prediction is used as the routing itself, so the staged set equals the routed set; an unmerged recovery LoRA repays the int4 and routing-replacement loss; 35B MoE at 20 tok/s in 3 GiB peak active memory on a 24 GB Mac mini M4 Pro | medium: replacing the router by a predictor changes the model (lossy, needs training); the idea of overlapping reads with the previous layer is ours already | read (setup extracted): Mac mini M4 Pro 24 GB, MLX |
| 2026-09-29 | [Mira, arXiv 2609.38090](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38090) (2 auth.) | Predictive expert staging with per-layer predictors plus adaptive caching and a tailored quantization format on a single GPU, against reactive offload systems | medium: another predictor-based prefetcher for the expert pool | read (setup extracted): RTX 6000 Ada 48 GB, RTX 3070 8 GB, Xeon w5-2565X |

### GitHub 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30 (`scan.py github`, complete pagination, window splitting)

Nine repositories (see the 2026-11-11 section). 12536 PRs (SGLang 4175, vLLM 3970, rocm-systems 1374, rocm-libraries 1140, llama.cpp 1049, TheRock 602, mlx 107, mlx-lm 90, ggml 29) and 1822 issues (by topic term) found, every one has a row in the screened book. Screening depth, honestly: of the four mandatory sources (llama.cpp, ggml, mlx, mlx-lm; 1648 titles) 1271 were read by title; **377 were not read**: the rows after the first 420 titles were pre-filtered by a noise filter (backend-only and housekeeping titles: Hexagon, OpenCL, SYCL, CANN, zDNN, WebGPU, OpenVINO, s390x/RISC-V, mtmd, vocab, jinja, UI, CI, docs, chat/tool-call parsers, convert, TTS/ASR, grammar/JSON, release and build chores) and those 377 are `drop` with `method` `keyword-filter`. vLLM, SGLang and the ROCm repositories: the keyword filter of the earlier months selected 627 titles, 150 of them were read; the other 12937 rows (including the 477 filter hits that were not read) are `drop`/`keyword-filter`. **Open for a later pass**: those 377 titles and the 477 unread vLLM/SGLang/ROCm filter hits (mostly hybrid-cache, ReplaySSM, DFlash and Qwen3.8 items). Numbers are the authors'; states from 2026-11-12.

## Backfill 2026-07 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2026-07-01 to 2026-07-31 of the backfill, scheme as in the Backfill 2026-10 section; deviation: **all** arXiv titles were read (no title filter), method per row in the `method` column of the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). The GitHub drops live in a local file (`work/papers/screened-github-drops.tsv`), the coverage book is the proof of completeness.

### arXiv 2026-07-01 to 2026-07-31

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 595 hits, complete. All 595 titles read; abstracts for the 51 that could matter; full text (HTML) for the five marked "read (full text)". Numbers are the authors'. 487 rows `drop` as `off-topic`.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-11 | [ATSInfer, arXiv 2607.10183](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10183) (4 auth.) | Hybrid CPU-GPU inference for **consumer devices** with offloading at **tensor** granularity (not layer or expert): static tensor placement plus load-aware dynamic transfer, asynchronous CPU-GPU coordination; the tensors of one layer differ strongly in size and backend time; prefill up to 1.94x, decode up to 3.29x over existing systems on dense and MoE models, better GPU and PCIe use | **high**: our weights exceed VRAM too; the load-aware part (placement follows the current CPU/GPU load) is exactly the case of **Tona next to work** (#116: the user's programs take CPU and GPU time while we run) | read (full text): RTX 3060 6 GB + i7-11800H laptop and RTX 4090 + i7-11700 desktop, DDR4, PCIe 4.0 x16; Qwen3-14B and Qwen3-30B-A3B; the baselines are llama.cpp-class and KTransformers-class systems, not our megakernel; rebuilding the graph (7 ms) and re-running the placement search (9 ms) per decision is as large as a token at 40 ms, so it only pays when load changes slowly |
| 2026-07-06 | [Elastic Gang, arXiv 2607.04668](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04668) (1 auth.) | The inference gang of a bare-metal x86-64 Rust kernel may change its core membership **between any two tokens** (ACK-latched epoch protocol, seqlock-style tag plus RCU consent, never waits on a named core); output bit-exact under per-token membership change (135M and 7B models, Zen 5 8C/16T); against static core splits it gives 1.75x / 1.52x / 1.28x the general throughput at 25 / 50 / 75 % inference duty at equal or better inference throughput | medium: the closest published answer to #116 (CPU threads of a barriered decode that must give cores back to the user's programs within a token) and the same CPU as ours; it is a custom kernel, not a user-space engine | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-13 | [LiteTopK, arXiv 2607.11976](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11976) (5 auth.) | Fused indexer-top-k for long-context sparse attention: score concentration lets a small sample estimate a threshold, scores are filtered while they are computed and only candidates are materialized (no full score matrix in HBM); LiteDSA reuses overlapping KV entries across neighbouring queries; integrated into vLLM, SGLang | medium: a model for a fused top-k indexer in a megakernel if we ever add DSA-style attention; NVIDIA datacenter evaluation | read (full text): data-centre GPUs, DSA models only |
| 2026-07-01 | [BaseRT, arXiv 2607.00501](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00501) (3 auth.); 2026-07-21 [BaseRT on M5, arXiv 2607.19438](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19438) (3 auth.; code basecompute/baseRT) | Native Metal runtime without framework layers: up to 1.56x decode over llama.cpp on M3/M4 Pro; second paper: hand-written Metal 4 tensor kernels route prefill GEMM (dense and MoE) and flash-attention prefill through the M5 neural accelerators, up to 6.4x prompt processing over llama.cpp and 3.9x over MLX, decode up to 1.75x / 1.33x, 15 models up to 35B on an M5 Pro | medium: the Mac-backend benchmark to beat; the same recipe as ours (no framework, fused kernels), and a hint that prefill is where the matrix units pay | read (full text): Apple M3/M4 Pro and M5 Pro; the comparison versions of llama.cpp and MLX are those of the authors |
| 2026-07-24 | [FusionML, arXiv 2607.22785](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22785) (1 auth.) | CPU+GPU co-execution on unified memory helps **prefill, not decode**: the MLX lazy-graph scheduler serializes cross-stream work unless an eager materialization boundary is set (row split 1.38x faster with materialized inputs, 0.66x slower inside a lazy graph); per-layer contention-aware split speeds prefill 1.15-1.38x, TTFT 1.18-1.25x on Qwen2.5-7B, decode unchanged because the shared bandwidth does not add; a no-regression gate becomes self-defeating under memory pressure | medium: states the bandwidth argument for our own CPU-offload decode; the memory-pressure note matters for #116 | read (full text): five Apple chips, three generations |
| 2026-07-19 | [Lossless but Not Free, arXiv 2607.17283](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17283) (1 auth.) | From-scratch speculative decoding on a consumer Apple laptop, five draft/target configurations; distribution equivalence verified at three levels (two-sample test over about 9,200 tokens, chi-square 162.5 at 200 dof, p 0.976, exact greedy agreement); the "lossless" speedup depends strongly on the backend pair | medium: a ready-made equivalence test for our speculation checks; consumer hardware | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-15 | [Quantize with Confidence?, arXiv 2607.14181](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14181) (3 auth.) | Six quantizers (GPTQ, AWQ, QuIP#, AQLM, bitsandbytes, GGUF) on Qwen2.5-Coder and CodeLlama, McEval and CoderEval Python/Java: pass@1 plus maintainability, reliability, security and structural complexity, robustness over prompt entropy and length | medium: code-quality view of quantization (our focus is coding); small models, no 1-2 bit | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-29 | [Flat Score, Amplified Failures, arXiv 2607.27275](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27275) (4 auth.) | tau2-bench, two open-weight families (dense and MoE), 16/8/4-bit, 456 episodes per cell: the score does not move (within +-7.5 points), but quantization amplifies the failure the model already has (tool-name hallucination) up to 2.5x (+17.6 points per task) | medium: confirms that a quantized agent model must be checked by trajectory and failure type, not by score alone | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-30 | [Agentic Coding in the Wild, arXiv 2608.00101](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00101) (6 auth.) | Sampled Copilot traces of June 2026 (3.2M users, 13M sessions, 761M LLM calls): sparse user turns, each an agent loop of calls coupled with tool execution; KV hit rate about 90 % inside a turn, 55 % across turn boundaries, invalidated by model switches and context compaction | medium: the first production-scale workload numbers for what Tona serves; supports keeping the prefix cache across turns and avoiding compaction | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-31 | [TokTier, arXiv 2607.29678](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29678) (2 auth.) | At a 94-99 % prompt-cache hit rate tokenization grows from 10 % to 64 % of TTFT; stateful tokenizer re-tokenizes only a window around the append and splices when a stability check passes, token IDs identical to full tokenization | medium: our tokenizer runs per request; the identical-IDs contract is the check to copy | new (abstract) |
| 2026-07-28 | [AngelSpec, arXiv 2607.25852](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25852) (13 auth.) | MTP drafter trained on chat data, block-diffusion drafter on code and math, chosen per workload | medium: argues for a code-specific drafter on coding workloads; needs training | new (abstract) |

Nine repositories. 9745 PRs (SGLang 2943, vLLM 2712, rocm-systems 1382, rocm-libraries 1117, llama.cpp 703, TheRock 601, mlx-lm 152, mlx 117, ggml 18) and 1613 issues (by topic term; vLLM 553, SGLang 323, llama.cpp 268, TheRock 173, rocm-systems 106, rocm-libraries 96, mlx 48, mlx-lm 44, ggml 2) found; every one has a row in the screened book. | 2026-06-20 | [WiSP, arXiv 2606.21868](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21868) (4 auth.; code nokia-applied-research/WiSP) | Low-resource MoE serving as a **working-set problem** of one VRAM budget shared by routed experts and KV cache: a routing-aware expert pager (full expert set pinned in host RAM, only routed experts paged into a small GPU scratch, LRU, stock fused-MoE kernel through an expert map, output byte-identical); up to 2.0x decode over static layer offload at the same budget when the model does not fit; **prefetching does not help in single-stream decode** because PCIe bandwidth, not prediction, is the limit (speculative transfers compete with demand transfers); MV-WSA splits the budget between experts and KV by marginal value | **high**: same regime as our expert streaming over PCIe 5 / DDR5; the "prediction does not help, allocation does" result is the argument for a residency policy (expert cache plus KV split) over a predictor, and it is a measured counter-claim to the many prefetch papers | read (full text): one 24 GiB RTX 3090 over PCIe 4.0 in vLLM, eager mode (no CUDA graphs), Python hot path; vLLM static offload is faster when host RAM suffices, an iso-VRAM test on a small card is left open; NVIDIA, no AMD |
| 2026-06-19 | [Does MoE Actually Help on Consumer and Edge Hardware?, arXiv 2606.21428](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21428) (5 auth.; code Analytics-Everywhere-Lab/edge-moe) | OLMoE-1B-7B against three dense baselines through llama.cpp on an M2 Pro 16 GB and a Jetson Orin Nano 8 GB: about 10 % behind the same-active-parameter dense model on the laptop, 31 % behind at 2.1x energy per token on the edge device; per-node timing shows routing under 9 % of the MoE block, the gap is total-parameter footprint, expert dispatch and KV pressure | medium: on bandwidth-bound hardware cost follows total, not active parameters; small model (1.3B active), no GPU-offload regime | read (partly): hardware and findings from the full text, only one MoE model |
