### arXiv 2026-04-01 to 2026-04-30

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`; the first run was incomplete after a 0/0 page and was repeated until complete): 620 hits, 609 not in this file before. **All 620 titles were read** (no title filter); abstracts for about 65 that could matter; full text (HTML) for the seven marked "read (full text)". Numbers are the authors'. 545 rows `drop` as `off-topic` by title.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-04-29 | [Efficient, VRAM-Constrained xLM Inference on Clients, arXiv 2604.26334](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26334) (4 auth., NVIDIA) | "Pipelined sharding": a profile-guided CPU-GPU scheduler for dense and MoE LLMs that do not fit in VRAM: sharding below layer level, CPU offload, pipelined copy-compute, prioritized tensor placement in VRAM; benchmark kernels are profiled at install time and the plan adapts to phase (prefill or decode), batch, context and concurrent GPU load; on a high-end desktop at batch 1 an average 2x TTFT (up to 6.7x) and up to 30x TPS over the stock llama.cpp split; implemented in llama.cpp, code released, upstreaming planned | **high**: the same problem and the same class of machine (discrete GPU on PCIe, DDR5 desktop); the install-time profile plus adaptation to a loaded GPU is the case of #116 (Tona next to work) and the closest published counterpart to our placement of tensors between VRAM and RAM | read (full text): the exact GPU and CPU of the "high-end desktop" were not extracted (NVIDIA authors, so NVIDIA cards); baseline is llama.cpp's layer split, not an expert-aware engine like ours; the 30x peak is for a model far larger than VRAM |
| 2026-04-14 | [Event Tensor, arXiv 2604.13327](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13327) (21 auth.) | Compiler abstraction for **dynamic megakernels**: dependencies between tiled tasks as a first-class symbolic-shape object (batch size and sequence length without recompilation or graph capture), static and dynamic scheduling of persistent kernels; Qwen3-30B-A3B: about 1.20x over SGLang at batch 1 and up to 1.09x at batch 64 (read from text fragments, the exact figures are in the paper's figures), the fused MoE layer ahead of FlashInfer GroupGEMM and Triton; shorter warm-up; already merged into a major open-source system | medium: a second published megakernel with per-task dependency counters (see ForgeMegakernel, Cohere, Mirage above), but compiled and on datacenter parts; the dynamic scheduling of MoE routing inside a persistent kernel is the part to compare with our expert path | read (full text): 8x NVIDIA B200 (NVLink), SGLang and vLLM with CUDA graphs and PDL as baselines, batch 1 to 128, prefill 512 and 100 output tokens |
| 2026-04-16 | [The Illusion of Equivalence, arXiv 2604.15409](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15409) (2 auth.) | KV-cached and cache-free decoding differ in the order of FP16 accumulation, which gives a deterministic token divergence under greedy decoding (100 % of runs on GSM8K for LLaMA-2-7B, Mistral-7B, Gemma-2-2B); FP32 removes it (flip rate 0.0 %); GQA models diverge from the first layer | medium: why "bit-exact" in `TONA_DETERMINISTIC` has to compare like with like (same cache path); our prefill and decode attention also accumulate in different orders (unchecked in the code) | read (full text): one H100 NVL, EPYC 9254, small dense models, FP16 |
| 2026-04-19 | [Layer-wise MoE Routing Locality under Shared-Prefix Code Generation, arXiv 2604.17182](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17182) (4 auth.) | Qwen3.5-35B-A3B-FP8 (256 experts, top-8), 851 code candidates branched from one prefix: expert sets of two sequences that emit the same token overlap with Jaccard 0.649 (40x random), with different tokens 0.175 (11x random); the middle layers (L14-20) dip for same tokens; 67 % of the compiled codes concentrate in a small set of branch points | medium: measures how predictable the experts are across candidates and layers for **code** on a model of our family; supports a per-layer preload policy, not a global one | read (full text): one GH200 (H100 96 GB, 120 GB LPDDR5X, NVLink-C2C); analysis only, no engine |
| 2026-04-14 | [DDTree, arXiv 2604.12989](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12989) (2 auth.) | A draft **tree** built from the per-position distributions of a block-diffusion drafter (DFlash): best-first heap under a node budget, provably maximizing the expected accepted length under the draft's surrogate, verified in one pass with an ancestor-only mask; reported ahead of vanilla DFlash | medium: our speculation branch is DFlash-like; trees cost verification width, which is what the experts of the verify pass pay for (see ECOSPEC, DraftExpert above) | read (full text): 8x H200, datacenter batch of requests; acceptance-length gains only, not on a single-user GPU |
| 2026-04-22 | [Temporally Extended MoE, arXiv 2604.20156](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20156) (2 auth.) | A controller per layer decides when to switch the expert set (option-critic with a deliberation cost); gpt-oss-20b with LoRA and a self-distillation reward: switch rate from over 50 % to below 5 %, keeping up to 90 % of the base accuracy on MATH and MMLU | medium: attacks the cause of our expert churn, but needs training and loses accuracy; the model family is not ours | read (full text): training on 4x H200; no offload measurement |
| 2026-04-28 | [DAK, arXiv 2604.26074](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26074) (3 auth.; code shouxulin/DirectAccessKernel) | Instead of prefetching weights into HBM, the GPU reads offloaded weights and KV straight from host memory into shared memory with the Tensor Memory Accelerator, with per-operation offload ratios, congestion control and multicast; up to 3x on GH200 (NVLink-C2C) and 1.8x on a PCIe RTX 6000 Blackwell; background copies slow down local memory by 4-7 % on a PCIe consumer card | medium: the case for direct reads over copy-then-compute on a PCIe link; the 4-7 % contention figure is relevant to our staging copies; TMA does not exist on RDNA4 | read (full text): GH200 and RTX 6000 Pro Blackwell, OPT and Llama dense models |
| 2026-04-18 | [Open-TQ-Metal, arXiv 2604.16957](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16957) (1 auth.) | Fused int4 KV attention in custom Metal shaders: 128K context for Llama 3.1 70B on a 64 GB Mac, 48x faster attention than dequantize-then-attend at 128K, KV 40 -> 12.5 GB, identical top-1 tokens to FP16; the attention scale (Gemma 4: attn_scale 1.0) decides whether angular KV quantizers survive | medium for the Mac backend (the int4 KV recipe); 330 experiments, Gemma 4 and Llama 3.1 only | new (abstract) |
| 2026-04-16 | [RACER, arXiv 2604.14885](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14885) (5 auth.; code hkr04/RACER) | Training-free drafting that joins retrieved exact patterns with logit-driven extrapolation; more than 2x on Spec-Bench, HumanEval, MGSM-ZH | medium: model-free drafting for coding agents, same family as suffix decoding; no hardware numbers in the abstract | new (abstract) |
| 2026-04-15 | [ToolSpec, arXiv 2604.13519](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13519) (5 auth.) | Tool-call traces are schema-constrained and repetitive: an FSM fills the fixed schema tokens deterministically and speculates only the variable fields, plus retrieval of similar earlier calls as drafts; up to 4.2x | medium: our workload is agent tool calls; the deterministic schema part needs grammar-aware decoding in the server | new (abstract) |
| 2026-04-20 | [Copy-as-Decode, arXiv 2604.18170](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18170) (1 auth.) | Edits as a two-primitive grammar (`<copy lines=i-j/>`, `<gen>`): copy spans are prefilled in parallel instead of decoded (6.8x-303x faster for 8-512 tokens, A100); an upper bound of 3.4x-29x wall-clock on code-edit sets, no end-to-end training | medium: an agent's edit tool re-emits large unchanged spans; a server-side copy primitive is cheap if the agent protocol allows it; upper bound only | new (abstract) |
| 2026-04-29 | [Quantamination, arXiv 2604.26505](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26505) (6 auth.) | Dynamic (per-batch) quantization of activations leaks information between requests in the same batch, an adversary can read other users' data; at least four popular frameworks affected | medium: a check for our 8-bit prefill and any batched activation quantization: scales must be per request, not per batch (unchecked in the code) | new (abstract) |

| Date | PR / issue | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-04-19 | [llama.cpp #22105](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/22105); 2026-04-26 [#22400](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/22400); 2026-04-30 [#22534](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/22534); 2026-04-26 [#22384](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/22384); 2026-04-09 [issue #21681](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/21681) | #22105 adds **DFlash** speculative decoding to llama.cpp ("up to 8x with Qwen3", numerically equal to the reference implementation, block-diffusion drafter instead of an autoregressive one such as EAGLE3); #22400 allows a **partial `seq_rm` for Gated DeltaNet models** by storing the GDN intermediates so a rejected draft rolls back up to `draft_max` instead of restarting from a checkpoint (the same idea as vLLM; CPU and CUDA only, breaking GDN API); #22534: for hybrid models `llama_memory_seq_rm` always fails on a partial range and the server then wipes all cached state, including the checkpoint it just restored; #22384: two bugs in the server's checkpoint search force a full re-prefill on every turn of Qwen3.5/3.6-27B (a 15K-token conversation costs about 15 s per turn instead of milliseconds); #21681 reports prompt-cache state drift in multi-turn use of Qwen3.5-35B-A3B | **high**: the state-rollback and checkpoint problem of our DeltaNet MTP and of multi-turn agent sessions, solved upstream in two different ways (stored intermediates, checkpoints); our DeltaNet state is F32 (`engine/gpu/mega_alloc.cpp`), so a copy per draft token is larger than with a smaller state type | #22105, #22400, #22534, #22384 closed; body read (500 chars), numbers not read in full |

| 2026-03-06 | [Persistent-State Dataflow Accelerator for GDN Decode, arXiv 2603.05931](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05931) (4 auth.) | Batch-1 Gated DeltaNet decode is memory-bound on GPUs because the 2 MB recurrent state must round-trip through HBM every token (arithmetic intensity about 1 FLOP/B, far below the H100 ridge point of about 25): the H100's L2 is a hardware-managed cache with no persistence guarantee across kernel launches; an FPGA keeps the whole state in on-chip memory, one read and one write pass per state matrix, 63 microseconds per token, 4.5x faster than the H100 | **high** as an argument: persistence of the recurrent state across tokens is the lever for DeltaNet decode; a megakernel is the GPU way to get it (state kept resident between layers and tokens instead of re-read); our state is F32 and its size per layer decides what fits on chip | read (abstract; text searched): AMD Alveo U55C (Vitis HLS) against an H100 PCIe, one model shape (Qwen3-Next-like), FPGA only |
| 2026-03-12 | [TaxBreak, arXiv 2603.12465](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12465) (6 auth.) | Decomposes the host-side cost of LLM inference into framework translation, CUDA library translation and kernel launch; a Host-Device Balance Index relates device-active time to host orchestration; per-kernel launch floor on H100/H200 | medium: the measurement method for "how much of a token is launch and sync", the quantity a megakernel removes; pairs with 2605.30571 | read (abstract; text searched): H100 and H200, PyTorch-level stacks |
| 2026-03-18 | [ESP, arXiv 2603.17942](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17942) (4 auth.) | Training-free multi-token prediction: probes the model with on-the-fly mask tokens from its embedding space, no weight change and no draft model | medium: a drafter-free speculation source if no MTP head exists; verification still needed | new (abstract) |
| 2026-03-02 | [Quasar, arXiv 2603.01399](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01399) (2 auth.) | Self-speculative decoding with a quantized verification pass to cut the memory cost of verification, which becomes the bottleneck once drafting is cheap | medium: verification reads the weights too; a lower-precision verifier is lossy unless corrected | new (abstract) |
| 2026-03-11 | [AgentServe, arXiv 2603.10342](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10342) (4 auth.) | Serving agent workloads (short reasoning-action loops, several agents) on a consumer-grade GPU: algorithm-system co-design for stable token emission and latency | medium: the consumer-GPU agent case; RTX A5000 and RTX 5090 with Qwen2.5-3B/7B and Llama-3-8B, NVIDIA | read (text searched) |
| 2026-03-09 | [The qs Inequality, arXiv 2603.08960](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08960) (2 auth.) | The double penalty of MoE at decode: routing fragments microbatches (less weight reuse), and the resident expert pool takes the HBM headroom from the KV cache | medium: a formula for when MoE loses to dense at small batch; fits the 2606.21428 measurements | new (abstract) |
| 2026-03-19 | [DyMoE, arXiv 2603.19172](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19172) (6 auth.) | Expert importance is highly skewed and depth-dependent; dynamic mixed-precision expert orchestration cuts memory and I/O for edge MoE inference | medium: per-expert bits driven by routing statistics (see NatScale, AlphaQ, GEMQ) | new (abstract) |
| 2026-03-24 | [CPU-Induced Slowdowns in Multi-GPU LLM Inference, arXiv 2603.22774](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22774) (4 auth.) | Under limited CPU allocation the GPUs idle because the CPU cannot keep them busy (4 H100 with 16 cores emulating a cloud allocation; also RTX Pro 6000) | medium: the same coupling in reverse for #116 (our host thread competes with the user's programs); multi-GPU datacenter setting | read (text searched) |

| 2026-01-29 | [ZipMoE, arXiv 2601.21198](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21198) (code: npnothard/ZipMoE-ICML26) | Semantically lossless on-device MoE serving: expert weights are compressed losslessly (statistical redundancy in the parameters), a cache-pool planning algorithm (expert-skew model plus dynamic programming) sets the memory budget per compression state, so decode turns from I/O-bound into compute-bound; up to 72.77 % lower latency and 6.76x throughput than the previous systems (authors' numbers, batch 1 to 16) | **high**: lossless compression of offloaded experts raises the effective PCIe/RAM bandwidth of our expert path without changing the numbers; a CPU/GPU decompression cost must pay for itself | read (text searched): Jetson AGX Orin 64 GB with SSD (unified memory), not a discrete GPU over PCIe |

## Backfill 2025-09 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2025-09-01 to 2025-09-30 of the backfill, scheme as in the Backfill 2026-07 section: all arXiv titles read, method per row in the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). Note for the whole 2025 block: Qwen3-Next (gated DeltaNet hybrid) and the first gated-delta kernels already exist in this month (released 2025-09-10), so the DeltaNet items below are the origin of that line of work, not late follow-ups.

### arXiv 2025-09-01 to 2025-09-30

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 399 hits, complete (the API answered 429 for about an hour while several scans shared one IP; the scan was repeated until complete, `ATTEMPTS` and the backoff of `papers_arxiv.py` were raised for that). All 399 titles read; abstracts for the 55 that could matter; HTML text skimmed only for hardware lines of the three offloading papers. Numbers are the authors'. 345 rows `drop` as `off-topic`.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-09-22 | [SubSpec, arXiv 2509.18344](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18344) (7 auth.; code NYCU-EDgeAi/subspec) | Lossless, training-free speculative decoding for **offloaded** targets: the draft model is built from low-bit quantized copies of the offloaded layers, shares the GPU-resident layers and the KV cache with the target (high alignment, long acceptance); 9.1x for Qwen2.5-7B with an 8 GB VRAM limit, 12.5x average for Qwen2.5-32B at 24 GB | medium: the same lever as our "draft from the weights already in VRAM" question for IQ1_M experts in RAM; the big numbers come from a baseline that streams every layer per token (1-2 tok/s), ours starts far higher | read (abstract; HTML skimmed): RTX 4090, i7-13700K, PCIe 4.0, batch 1, dense Qwen2.5 models only, no MoE |
| 2025-09-10 | [MoEpic, arXiv 2509.08342](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08342) (4 auth.) | Expert **split**: each expert is cut vertically in two segments; the top segment of hot experts stays in VRAM (more experts fit, higher hit rate), the bottom is prefetched per layer from the next-layer prediction; per-layer VRAM budget and split ratio by fixed-point iteration; half the GPU cost, 37.5-65.7 % lower latency than the baselines | medium: a finer unit than whole experts for the VRAM cache; needs a router-based predictor | read (abstract; HTML skimmed): Xeon 8358P workstation with A6000-class GPUs, PCIe 4.0, own Python system, no code link found |
| 2025-09-28 | [LayerScope / PreScope, arXiv 2509.23638](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23638) (9 auth.) | Learned layer-aware expert predictor, globally planned prefetch against loading cost, asynchronous I/O decoupled from compute for multi-batch MoE on PCIe-bound commodity servers; 141 % more throughput, 74.6 % lower latency than the best prior system | medium: prediction-driven prefetch; the multi-batch focus is not our single-stream decode | read (abstract; HTML skimmed): V100 and A100, batched |
| 2025-09-09 | [DuoServe-MoE, arXiv 2509.07379](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07379) (5 auth.) | Separate policies per phase: prefill overlaps expert prefetch with compute, decode uses a layer-level predictor trained offline from activation traces; TTFT up to 5.34x, end-to-end up to 7.55x over baselines at low peak GPU memory | medium: prefill/decode split matches our staging idea; trained predictor needs traces per model | new (abstract) |
| 2025-09-27 | [Bridging the Gap Between Promise and Performance for Microscaling FP4, arXiv 2509.23202](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23202) (11 auth.) | MXFP4 and NVFP4 post-training study: NVFP4's small groups defeat outlier tricks, MXFP4's power-of-two scales cost accuracy; MR-GPTQ (block-wise Hadamard, fused into the weights, fast online rotation for activations) closes most of the gap; end-to-end 2.2x vs FP16 on B200, 4x on RTX 5090 | medium: the recipe if MXFP4 weights ever matter on gfx1200 (the abstract names AMD GPUs as format-capable; the kernels are NVIDIA) | read (abstract): B200, RTX 5090 |
| 2025-09-24 | [Q-Palette, arXiv 2509.20214](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20214) (2 auth.; code snu-mllab/Q-Palette) | Fractional-bit weight quantizers (trellis-coded down to scalar) with CUDA kernels, plus a mixed-scheme search over quantizer choice and layer fusion under a resource budget | medium: bit allocation by budget is how our mixed IQ quants are chosen by hand | new (abstract) |

Notes (abstract or title only, rows in the book): 2509.01083 (DSDE, dynamic draft length), 2509.20416 (FastEagle, one-pass drafter), 2509.22134, 2509.24328 (drafter training, verification), 2509.04185 (set block decoding), 2509.07727 (error-bounded lossy compression of offloaded experts), 2509.02753, 2509.10377, 2509.22299, 2509.19781, 2509.25689, 2509.22951 (MoE pruning, merging, edge compression; most need calibration), 2509.02512 (MoPEQ, per-expert bits), 2509.01229 (LiquidGEMM, W4A8 kernel), 2509.22944 (SINQ), 2509.16989, 2509.23809, 2510.03267 (ternary), 2509.14391, 2510.00028 (RoPE scaling under quantization), 2509.24626, 2509.24745, 2509.24663 (sparse attention), 2509.21081 (MLA kernel for shared prefixes), 2509.22630 (state expansion for recurrent models), 2509.05282, 2509.18467 (linear attention), 2509.04377, 2509.17396, 2510.00231 (KV eviction; the last one a warning), 2509.09505 (agentic long-context memory walls), 2509.03377 (CXL tier), 2509.07506 (GPU kernel agent).

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-11-11 | [HipKittens, arXiv 2511.08083](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08083) (9 auth., Hazy Research; code HazyResearch/HipKittens) | Tile-based kernel DSL for AMD: the ThunderKittens abstractions carry over to AMD, but the algorithms differ (8-wave ping-pong and 4-wave interleave schedules instead of wave specialization, which reaches only 80 % of BF16 GEMM peak on MI355X; chiplet-aware cache/grid scheduling for the 8 XCDs); validated on CDNA3 MI325X and CDNA4 MI355X, competes with hand-written assembly for GEMM and attention and is 1.2-2.4x faster than all baselines in some settings (d=64 attention, GQA backward, memory-bound kernels); AITER reaches only 30 % / PyTorch 24 % on GQA backward | medium-high: the same lesson for our hand-written HIP kernels (scheduling primitives matter more than compute abstractions), but CDNA only, no RDNA | new (read full text) |
| 2025-11-10 | [Pre-Attention Expert Prediction and Prefetching for MoE LLMs, arXiv 2511.10676](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10676) (4 auth.) | Predicts the experts of layer l from the activations **before the attention block of the same layer** with two linear functions and a ranking-aware loss (ranking-preserving property), so it also works for the first layer; 93.03 % accuracy on DeepSeek-V2-Lite, 94.69 % on Qwen3-30B, 97.62 % on Phi-mini-MoE, about +15 points over the best earlier predictor; evaluated on A100 | **high** for expert preloading: a cheap predictor that gives the experts one attention block earlier than the router input of the previous layer; needs a trained predictor per model and not our model (Qwen3-Next-like) tested | new (read full text) |
| 2025-11-14 | [KVSwap, arXiv 2511.11907](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11907) (3 auth.) | Whole KV cache on disk, compact in-memory metadata predicts which entries to preload, disk reads shaped to the storage device and overlapped with compute; higher throughput than other KV offloading under tight memory, quality kept | medium: the SSD tier of the KV cache for long contexts on a RAM-limited desktop; abstract only | new (abstract) |
| 2025-11-15 | [Cacheback, arXiv 2511.21699](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21699) (3 auth.) | Training-free drafting from **LRU tables of token n-grams** only; reported best among comparable model-free methods | medium: same family as our suffix drafts; no hardware numbers in the abstract | new (abstract) |
| 2025-11-21 | [Deterministic Inference across Tensor Parallel Sizes, arXiv 2511.17826](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17826) (7 auth.); [DiFR, 2511.20621](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20621) | Batch-invariant kernels exist, but the same greedy output across TP sizes needs a fixed reduction order too; DiFR verifies an inference provider by comparing tokens with a reference run under the same seed despite numerical noise | medium: both frame `TONA_DETERMINISTIC` (reduction order is the lever, nondeterminism budget for verification); no GPU of our class | new (abstract) |
| 2025-11-27 | [PAT, arXiv 2511.22333](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22333) (10 auth.) | Prefix-aware decode attention: pack queries that share a prefix so the shared KV is loaded once, multi-tile kernel with uneven-length handling | low-medium: needs several concurrent requests with shared prefixes; we mostly decode one sequence | new (abstract) |
| 2025-11-23 | [Kitty, arXiv 2511.18643](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18643) (15 auth.) | 2-bit KV cache: only a small fraction of the most sensitive key channels is boosted to 4 bit, stored as two uniform 2-bit tensors per page so dequantization stays uniform | medium: a lossy KV path with long-context reasoning quality kept; abstract only | new (abstract) |
| 2025-11-04 | [Apriel-H1, arXiv 2511.02651](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02651) (13 auth.) | Distils a 15B reasoning transformer into hybrids by replacing the least critical attention layers with Mamba blocks step by step; a 30/50 variant gives over 2x throughput in vLLM with little loss | low-medium: a recipe for turning a dense model into a hybrid; the engine side is the same recurrent state | new (abstract) |

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-10-08 | [OWL, arXiv 2510.07535](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07535) (6 auth.) | Speculative decoding on **long contexts**: existing drafters degrade with context length, EAGLE3 even slows generation to 0.81x; a new benchmark (LongSpecBench) and a drafter that reaches about 5x EAGLE3's acceptance length on long inputs; trained on 8xH200, evaluated with batch size 1 on H200 | **high** for agent work with long prompts: a measured case where a trained drafter loses at long context; no consumer GPU | new (read full text) |
| 2025-10-30 | [ExpertFlow, arXiv 2510.26730](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26730) (6 auth.) | MoE offloading with adaptive cross-layer prediction depth (`numSteps`): on A6000 (PCIe 64 GB/s) 2 steps remove most waiting, slower links (RTX 4090 at 32 GB/s) need more steps to overlap transfer and compute; latency varies up to 300 % at equal batch size with the semantic diversity of the tokens; Table 1 lists link bandwidths from 128 GB/s (H20) down to 8 GB/s (RX 6500 XT) | medium-high: prediction depth versus link speed, our link is PCIe 5.0 x16, measured 55.7 GB/s per direction (docs/backends/reference-platform.md); the predictor is part of the system | new (read full text) |
| 2025-10-07 | [The Anatomy of a Triton Attention Kernel, arXiv 2511.11581](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11581) (4 auth.; vLLM) | A paged-attention kernel written only in Triton that runs on NVIDIA (H100) and AMD (MI300) at state-of-the-art speed; small batches launch few program instances and underuse the GPU; vLLM records CUDA graphs for power-of-two batch sizes up to 128 | medium: portable attention across vendors; CDNA only, no RDNA | new (read full text) |
| 2025-10-15 | [NOSA, arXiv 2510.13602](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13602) (12 auth.) | Trainable sparse attention designed for KV offloading: the KV accesses are constrained so CPU-GPU transfers stay small; training-free offloading loses quality on long generation | medium: needs a model trained with it | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-26 | [Correctness Forensics for Batch Speculative Decoding, arXiv 2510.22876](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22876) (6 auth.) | Several widely used batch speculative implementations silently produce corrupted output (repeated tokens, `<unk>`) at competitive speed; root cause: variable acceptance desynchronizes position IDs, attention masks and KV cache across the batch; formal invariants (rectangular alignment, contiguous position IDs) | medium: a checklist for our verify and rollback path (single sequence here, but the invariants apply per sequence) | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-07 | [Patterns behind Chaos, arXiv 2510.05497](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05497) (9 auth.) | Profiling of expert-selection data movement over 24 000 requests on four 200B-1000B MoE models, temporal and spatial patterns for forecasting it | low-medium: multi-unit serving scale, but the temporal statistics are comparable data for expert preloading | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-15 | [Mirror-SD, 2510.13161](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13161); [HiSpec, 2510.01336](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01336); [SPEQ, 2510.18525](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18525); [SpecAttn, 2510.27641](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27641); [CAS-Spec, 2510.26843](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26843) | Draft-side variants: early-exit rollouts in parallel to the target (Mirror-SD), cheap intermediate verification (HiSpec: verification can be 4x slower than generation), a quantized draft from part of the target's own weight bits (SPEQ), sparse attention for the target from the draft's attention weights (SpecAttn), cascaded self-speculation on the fly (CAS-Spec) | low-medium: abstract only; SPEQ and SpecAttn are the ones that need no separate draft model | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-08 | [LMCache, arXiv 2510.09665](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09665) (11 auth.) | Open-source KV cache layer that stores and transfers KV outside the GPU, reuse across queries and engines, enterprise scale | low-medium: tiered KV storage design; serving scale | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-22 | [Ring-linear 2.0, arXiv 2510.19338](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19338) (28 auth.); [Hybrid Architectures for Language Models, 2510.04800](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04800) | Hybrid linear/softmax attention model series (16B-A0.96B and 104B-A6.1B, inference cost 1/10 of a dense 32B) and a systematic comparison of inter-layer versus intra-layer hybrids | low-medium: model-class context for the recurrent layers | new (abstract) |
| 2025-10-07 | [vAttention, arXiv 2510.05688](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05688) (8 auth.); [AMD MI300X Performance Analysis, 2510.27583](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27583); [Production-Grade Local LLM Inference on Apple Silicon, 2511.05502](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.05502) | Sparse attention that combines top-k and sampling with a quality guarantee; MI300X inference analysis; MLX, MLC-LLM, llama.cpp, Ollama and PyTorch MPS compared on an M2 Ultra with 192 GB up to 100K-token prompts | low-medium: the Apple study is the only broad cross-runtime comparison at long context on Apple silicon | new (abstract) |

Gap closure 2025-06-01 to 2025-06-30 of the backfill, scheme as in the Backfill 2026-07 section: all arXiv titles read, method per row in the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). GitHub as in the Backfill 2026-04 section: all llama.cpp, ggml, MLX and mlx-lm titles read; vLLM, SGLang and the ROCm repositories by keyword (the first list of the earlier backfills plus a second, wider list: gfx11/gfx12, RDNA, WMMA, HIP, ROCm, MoE, expert, offload, spec, draft, MTP, EAGLE, quant, FP8/FP4, attention, MLA, DeltaNet, Mamba/SSM, hybrid, KV cache, prefix cache, mmap, tool call, grammar; titles with CI, docs, test, TPU, Gaudi, NPU and similar markers were skipped even when a keyword matched); the rest is `keyword-filter` in the book.

### arXiv 2025-06-01 to 2025-06-30

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 349 hits, 347 not in this file before (the topic query returns far fewer hits for 2025 than for 2026). **All 349 titles were read**; abstracts for about 60; full text (HTML, search for hardware, batch size, code links) for the three marked "read (full text)". Numbers are the authors'. The first two rows are the only ones that concern our hardware class directly.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-06-17 | [Utility-Driven Speculative Decoding for MoE ("Cascade"), arXiv 2506.20675](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20675) (5 auth.) | For a MoE, the draft tokens of one verify pass together touch more experts, so verification moves 2-3x more weight data and speculation with a static K slows decoding down by up to 1.5x (n-gram drafting on Mixtral); Cascade measures a "speculation utility" (token gain divided by verify cost), switches speculation off per request when it drops below 1 in a test phase and hill-climbs K when it is above 1; in vLLM, five MoEs (Mixtral, OLMoE, DeepSeek-V1, Qwen-1.5 MoE and others), code, math, extraction and mixed workloads: worst slowdown 5 % instead of 1.5x, 7-14 % more throughput than static K; also run with EAGLE | **high**: the exact problem of speculative decoding on our MoE with offloaded experts (a wider verify touches more experts, which cost PCIe or RAM time, not just HBM time), and a cheap online rule for when and how wide to speculate, which an agent workload with changing text (code, tool output) needs | read (full text, HTML): RTX 6000 Ada (48 GB) and i9-14900K with 64 GB RAM, so a workstation card, **no offload** (all experts in VRAM, the cost is HBM traffic); vLLM; batch not stated in the passages read; no code link found in the text |
| 2025-06-03 | [APEX, arXiv 2506.03296](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03296) (4 auth.) | Profiling-informed scheduler that overlaps CPU-side attention on offloaded KV with GPU work in decode when GPU memory limits the batch; CPU attention at batch 4 takes 3031 us against 170 us on the GPU (hidden 2048, 1024 tokens, V100 and two EPYC 7251), i.e. under 10 % of GPU throughput, so the schedule predicts execution times instead of using static rules; throughput +84 to 96 % (T4) and +11 to 89 % (A10) over vLLM, up to 72 % and 37 % over the best hybrid schedulers in long-output settings | medium: shows how small the CPU attention share is on a slow CPU; Zen 5 with AVX-512 and DDR5 is a different case, and our KV stays in VRAM (unchecked: no code path in the engine offloads attention) | read (full text, HTML): T4 and A10, LLaMA-2-7B and LLaMA-3.1-8B, dense models, throughput and batch focus, no MoE |
| 2025-06-25 | [LeoAM, arXiv 2506.20187](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20187) (4 auth.) | Long-context KV on one commodity GPU with a GPU-CPU-disk hierarchy: variable-size chunks from the skewed attention distribution, a small "KV abstract" per chunk stored on disk instead of the full chunk, compression and pipelining; average latency 3.46x better than prior systems at comparable quality | medium-low: the 150K-token Llama-2-13B case does not fit RTX 4090 plus 120 GB RAM, the same pressure as our 1M-context goal; but it selects KV by importance (lossy) and goes to disk | read (full text, HTML, hardware lines only): RTX 4090 and 4060 class, 2K to 150K context, Llama-2 7B and 13B, batch 1 and 4 |
| 2025-06-05 | [Log-Linear Attention, arXiv 2506.04761](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04761) (6 auth.) | Replaces the fixed-size state of linear attention by a logarithmically growing set of states with a matmul-rich parallel form at log-linear cost; instantiated on Mamba-2 and **Gated DeltaNet**, which then perform well against their linear-time variants | medium: a possible successor of the Qwen3-Next state, but it needs trained models and a growing state (our DeltaNet state is F32 and fixed, `engine/gpu/mega_alloc.cpp`); no model release for it known to us | new (abstract) |
| 2025-06-01 | [Mamba Drafters for Speculative Decoding, arXiv 2506.01206](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01206) (12 auth.) | A Mamba SSM as an external drafter: no quadratic cost in the draft, less memory than self-speculation, works across target models, plus a test-time tree search; on par with state-of-the-art self-speculation | medium: a constant-size drafter state suits a draft that runs inside a persistent kernel; needs a trained Mamba drafter for our model family (not available) | new (abstract) |

Notes (one line each, abstract only unless noted, rows in the book): 2506.22694 (VocabTrim, already listed above), 2506.03206 (out-of-vocabulary sampling for small-vocabulary drafters), 2506.07900 (MiniCPM4: trainable sparse attention, a CPM.cu inference system with speculation and quantization, BitCPM ternary, on-device), 2506.22776 (quantized code LLMs are not less robust than full precision, 350M to 33B), 2506.11309 (SwiftSpec: asynchronous speculative decoding plus tensor parallelism, multi-GPU), 2506.13585 (MiniMax-M1: hybrid lightning-attention MoE, 456B), 2506.10443 (MNN-LLM, mobile), 2506.04708 (STAND, n-gram drafting for test-time scaling), 2506.01151 (Earley-based pruning for grammar-constrained decoding, relevant to tool calls), 2506.02523 (MLA hardware analysis), 2506.08373 (draft-model guided KV and prompt selection, lossy), 2506.17121 (KV footprint metric for eviction methods), 2506.12355 (generated attention kernels), 2506.22169 (MCFuser fusion of memory-bound compute-intensive operators), 2506.24045 (Agent.xpu: agent flow scheduling on CPU+iGPU+NPU SoCs), 2506.09554 (LLM inference on a Jetson Orin AGX 64 GB), 2506.23025 (ternary models), 2506.11728 (survey of mixed-precision matmul), 2506.04108 and 2506.08889 (sparse attention, lossy or needs training), 2506.12417 and 2506.07366 (multi-GPU MoE load balancing, datacenter).

### arXiv 2025-05-01 to 2025-05-31

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 480 hits, 476 not in this file before. **All 480 titles were read** (about 120 with an obvious other domain, such as time series, medical, EEG, quantum, video, speech, finance, federated, were skimmed in a short form); abstracts for about 45; full text (HTML, hardware, batch size and code-link passages) for the six marked "read (full text)". Numbers are the authors'.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-05-09 | [FloE, arXiv 2505.05950](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05950) (8 auth.) | MoE offloading where the PCIe transfer of an expert (an expert computes in about 5 ms on an RTX 3090 but moves much slower over the bus) is the bottleneck: compresses the internal matrices of each expert (9.3x per expert on Mixtral-8x7B; the compression techniques themselves were not extracted from the text) and uses a cheap sparse predictor to load only what is needed; runs Mixtral-8x7B in 11 GB of VRAM (8.5x less memory), 48.7x faster than DeepSpeed-MII at 4.4 to 7.6 % average accuracy loss | **high**: the same bottleneck as our expert path (bytes per expert over PCIe and from RAM), a lossy remedy (compression on top of the model's own quantization) that needs the quality check of AGENTS.md, and the 48.7x is against a weak baseline, not an expert-aware engine | read (full text): GeForce RTX 3090 (24 GB), Mixtral-8x7B, ShareGPT prompts, batch 1 latency; code link not extracted |
| 2025-05-15 | [SpecOffload, arXiv 2505.10259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10259) (9 auth.; code MobiSense/SpecOffload-public) | Puts speculative decoding into the offloading pipeline: GPU cores idle while weights stream in, so the free VRAM and cores hold and run a draft model; target and draft run interleaved, a planner places tensors and picks the parameters; 4.49x higher GPU core utilization and 2.54x throughput against the best baseline, aimed at high-throughput (large-batch) offline inference | **high** for the idea (spare VRAM and idle cores during a transfer-bound phase run the draft; relevant to our MTP or draft work), but the setting is throughput with large batches, not one agent at batch 1; the draft is a separate model | read (full text): consumer-class setup (an RTX 4090 with PCIe 4.0 is the worked example of the introduction; exact evaluation GPU, models and batch not extracted); code released |
| 2025-05-26 | [MoESD, arXiv 2505.19645](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19645) (8 auth.) | Speculative decoding helps a sparse MoE **more** than a dense model at medium batch sizes: once the batch is big enough that every expert is active anyway, verifying several draft tokens costs no extra expert loading; the sparser the MoE, the wider that batch range; a model and a "target efficiency" metric explain why a high acceptance rate can still give no speedup; up to 2.29x for Qwen2-57B-A14B at medium batch | **high**: the opposite regime to Cascade (2025-06, above in this file: a static K slows MoEs down at small batch); both together say that our single-user, batch-1 verify width is exactly where extra draft tokens cost experts, so the tree width must be chosen against the expert cache (the same quantity ECOSPEC and DraftExpert work on, see above) | read (full text): "different GPUs" (not extracted), Qwen2-57B-A14B and other MoEs, batch sizes from small to medium; no offload |
| 2025-05-21 | [Not All Models Suit Expert Offloading, arXiv 2505.16056](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16056) (6 auth.; code ljcleo/moe-lrc) | Two metrics for how consistent consecutive tokens' experts are (segment routing best performance, segment cache best hit rate) over 20 MoE LLMs; strong trade-off between local routing consistency and local load balance, shared experts lower consistency, domain-specialized experts contribute more than vocabulary-specialized ones; the best cache size is about twice the number of active experts | **high**: gives the size of an expert cache per layer (2x active experts) and a way to measure our target model's routing before we size anything; the models measured are not our Qwen3-Next family (unchecked which of the 20 are) | read (full text): analysis of released models plus toy models trained on OLMoE data; no engine |
| 2025-05-20 | [STree, arXiv 2505.14969](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14969) (4 auth.; code wyc1997/stree) | Tree-based speculative decoding for state-space and hybrid SSM-attention models: the accumulated state-transition matrices let a token tree be verified with little overhead against sequential verification; a hardware-aware kernel; beats vanilla speculative decoding with SSMs even with a baseline draft model and tree on three benchmarks | **high** for the DeltaNet layers: verifying K draft tokens or a tree through a recurrent layer and keeping a per-branch state is our MTP-verify problem; the method is for Mamba-style (diagonal) transitions, whether it carries over to the delta-rule update is unchecked | read (full text): RTX 3090, H100 in the appendix, MambaInLlama-8B (50 % transformer blocks) and Mamba-style models, no Qwen3-Next |
| 2025-05-28 | [FlashFormer, arXiv 2505.22758](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22758) (7 auth.) | Fuses the whole transformer forward pass into one kernel for low-batch inference where bandwidth and launch overhead dominate; generated by a metaprogramming language that partitions work statically; speedups over GPT-Fast and vLLM at batch 1 across sizes and quantizations ("nontrivial", exact figures not extracted) | **high** as a megakernel reference next to ForgeMegakernel, Mirage and the Hazy blog above: static workload partitioning at batch 1; NVIDIA only | read (full text): one H100 SXM, batch 1 and larger, dense models; baselines GPT-Fast and vLLM |
| 2025-05-04 | [An Empirical Study of Qwen3 Quantization, arXiv 2505.02214](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02214) (10 auth.) | Five classic post-training quantization methods applied to Qwen3 at 1 to 8 bits on several datasets: competitive at moderate bit widths, notable degradation on linguistic tasks at ultra-low precision (numbers not read) | medium: early data on how low-bit quantization hits Qwen3, the family of our model; GGUF K and I quants are probably not among the methods (unchecked) | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-28 | [Hardware-Efficient Attention for Fast Decoding, arXiv 2505.21487](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21487) (3 auth.) | Grouped-Tied Attention (reuses and combines key and value states, matches GQA quality with about half the KV cache) and Grouped Latent Attention (matches MLA, easier to shard), more compute per byte of KV loaded | medium: an architecture choice, not something to retrofit; needs models trained that way | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-12 | [LongCodeBench, arXiv 2505.07897](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07897) (8 auth.) | Benchmark of coding LLMs at 1M tokens of context (code comprehension and repair) | medium: a ready workload for the 1M-context target with code | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-28 | [EFIM, arXiv 2505.21889](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21889) (7 auth.) | In fill-in-the-middle prompts the KV cache of the prefix or the suffix is invalidated as the other part grows; EFIM transforms the prompt format to allow reuse and adds training against the sub-token generation problem this causes | medium: fill-in-the-middle is a coding use; the reuse problem is a prompt-format one | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-21 | [BanditSpec, arXiv 2505.15141](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15141) (9 auth.) | Training-free online choice of the speculative-decoding hyperparameters while text is generated, as a multi-armed bandit (UCBSpec, EXP3Spec) with regret bounds | medium: the same control question as Cascade's utility rule, in a cleaner theory; only dense setups | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-28 | [Speculative Decoding Meets Quantization, arXiv 2505.22179](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22179) (7 auth.) | With EAGLE-2 on 4-bit weight-quantized targets, verifying a tree-style draft takes much longer than a single-token pass, so the memory benefit of quantization is eaten by the verify compute; a hierarchical scheme with a small intermediate model turns tree drafts into sequence drafts | medium: relevant to a verify pass on IQ1/IQ2 weights, where dequantization cost grows with the verify width (the effect is not tested here) | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-12 | [Overflow Prevention Enhances Long-Context Recurrent LLMs, arXiv 2505.07793](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07793) (7 auth.) | Recurrent sub-quadratic models underuse long context because of the fixed-size memory; processing only the relevant chunks (chunk-based inference) mitigates it | medium: a caution for the 1M-context target on the DeltaNet layers (fixed state, F32 in `engine/gpu/mega_alloc.cpp`) | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-01 | [ML Drift, arXiv 2505.00232](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00232) (8 auth.) | On-device GPU inference engine for generative models with 10 to 100x more parameters than earlier on-device ones, across mobile and desktop GPU APIs, an order of magnitude over existing open-source engines (per the abstract; the baselines were not read) | medium: Apple GPU path ideas; mostly diffusion and mobile | new (abstract) |
| 2025-05-29 | [Ghidorah, arXiv 2505.23219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23219) (6 auth.) | Speculative decoding plus hetero-core model parallelism: the workload is partitioned across the CPU and GPU cores of a unified-memory device, with a profiling step that picks the speculative and partitioning strategy | medium: unified-memory SoCs (Apple, Strix-class), not a discrete GPU | new (abstract) |

Notes (one line each, rows in the book): 2505.01572 (PipeSpec, pipelined hierarchy of draft models), 2505.06461 (CPU outperforms the GPU for a 1B model on an iPhone 15 Pro via llama.cpp), 2505.16968 (CASS: CUDA to HIP and SASS to RDNA3 translation dataset and models), 2505.03804 (MoEQuant), 2505.07608 (MiMo, an MTP-trained reasoning model), 2505.07858 (scaling laws for speculative decoding), 2505.07203 (engine for prefill-only workloads), 2505.17272 (Zebra-Llama hybrid models from pretrained ones), 2505.15431 (Hunyuan-TurboS Mamba-transformer MoE), 2505.09343 (DeepSeek-V3 hardware reflections), 2505.17694 (CoDec prefix-shared decoding kernel), 2505.20276 and 2505.19433 (quantization and long context, quantization and agentic ability), 2505.23786 (attack that hides a malicious model in GGUF quantization error), 2505.06708 (gated attention, already listed above), 2505.11594 (SageAttention3, already listed above), 2505.09388 (Qwen3 technical report, already listed above).

Eight repositories. 2318 PRs: vLLM 955, SGLang 595, llama.cpp 400, TheRock 160, rocm-libraries 86, MLX 50, mlx-lm 41, ggml 31. **llama.cpp, ggml, MLX and mlx-lm: all 522 titles read**; bodies (500 characters) for the rows below; others by keyword (about 480 titles read). States are from 2026-11-12.

| Date | PR | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-05-08 | [llama.cpp #13386](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/13386) (issue [#13241](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/13241)) | `--no-op-offload`: with `-ot exps=CPU` (experts in RAM, rest on the GPU) the backend offloads large-batch matmuls of CPU-resident experts to the GPU by copying the weights, which makes prefill very slow; the issue's measurements (Llama 4 Maverick 400B Q4_0, ROCm build, `llama-bench`, GPU not named): pp512 23.1 t/s at the default ubatch 512, 93.7 t/s at ubatch 16 (which bypasses the offload), 233.7 t/s with the offload disabled in the source, tg128 unchanged at about 26.7 to 26.9 t/s | **high**: the prefill rate of experts that live in RAM; either the CPU computes them or the weights cross PCIe, and a bad switch costs 10x; our expert path faces the same decision per batch size (a measured crossover) | closed (merged); body and issue read |
| 2025-05-29 | [llama.cpp #13904](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/13904) (and #13746, #13276, #13706, #13808) | Hybrid recurrent cache in its final form: two explicit child caches (unified KV and recurrent), the memory-state interface (#13746) and the KV-cell rework underneath it | medium: the upstream structure for attention plus SSM state in one context; second step after #13979 (June) | closed; body read (500 chars) |

| 2025-05-19 | [llama.cpp #13639](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/13639) (with #13607, #13750, #13101 in April) | GLM4 gives incoherent output with F16 accumulators on Vulkan; the fixes use F32 accumulators in the quantized matmul and in the attention output (April: F32 compute for the FFN down projection) | medium: the same family of overflow traps as the Metal flash-attention fix of June (#13975, above); where our kernels accumulate in half precision is unchecked here | closed; body read (500 chars) |

### arXiv 2025-04-01 to 2025-04-30

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 265 hits, 262 not in this file before. **All 265 titles were read** (about 90 with an obvious other domain skimmed in a short form); abstracts for about 25; full text (HTML, hardware and code-link passages) for the four marked "read (full text)". Numbers are the authors'.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-04-08 | [HybriMoE, arXiv 2504.05897](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05897) (6 auth.; code PKU-SEC-Lab/HybriMoE) | Hybrid CPU-GPU MoE inference on top of kTransformers: a dynamic intra-layer scheduler balances expert work between CPU and GPU (activation patterns are unstable, so a fixed expert-to-device map fails), an impact-driven inter-layer prefetch, and a score-based expert cache; on average 1.33x in prefill and 1.70x in decode over the best hybrid framework (kTransformers) | **high**: the closest published counterpart to expert placement between VRAM and RAM with the CPU computing the experts that are not resident, including the cache policy; consumer-class evaluation | read (full text): NVIDIA RTX A6000 (48 GB), three MoE LLMs (names not extracted), baselines kTransformers and llama.cpp, code released |
| 2025-04-16 | [MOM, arXiv 2504.12526](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12526) (4 auth.; code TianyiZhu877/MOM) | Long-context prefill memory: the MLP and the last layer's logits over the whole sequence dominate peak memory, so critical layers run on "mini-sequences" together with KV offloading; peak memory down by more than 50 % on average, on Llama-3.2-8B the maximum context grows from 155K to 455K tokens on one A100 80 GB with identical outputs, 35 % more extension than chunked prefill | **high** for the 1M-context target: the intermediates of prefill, not only the KV cache, bound the context on a 16 GB card; exact outputs, so it passes the bit-exact rule if the arithmetic order is kept (unchecked); needs checking against our prefill chunking | read (full text): A100 80 GB, batch 1, bfloat16, Llama, Qwen and Mistral 8B-class models; code released |
| 2025-04-07 | [Prima.cpp, arXiv 2504.08791](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08791) (11 auth.; code OpenCPIL/prima.cpp) | llama.cpp-based system for 30-70B models over a home cluster of mixed CPUs and GPUs with too little RAM and VRAM and slow disks: pipelined-ring parallelism overlaps disk I/O, compute and communication, fixes the prefetch-release conflict of mmap-based offloading, and a scheduler ("Halda") assigns layers per device; 70B at 674 ms per token with under 6 % memory pressure on four devices, a 32B model with speculative decoding at 26 tokens/s, 5 to 17x lower TPOT than llama.cpp, exo and dllama | medium: the mmap prefetch and release conflict is the trap of running a model larger than RAM from disk, relevant to our RAM-pressure work (#116); multi-device and Wi-Fi parts do not apply | read (full text, hardware lines only): four consumer devices with mixed CPU and GPU (an RTX 2080 Ti device is the most capable one), dense 30-70B models |
| 2025-04-17 | [D2MoE, arXiv 2504.15299](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15299) (4 auth.) | On-device MoE: each expert is stored as nested bit planes ("matryoshka" weight quantization, bit-nested), the bit width per expert is chosen at run time by a router and a "hottest-expert-bit-first" I/O-compute scheduler; up to 1.39x throughput and 53 % less peak memory against the latest on-device frameworks at accuracy comparable to their INT8 counterparts | medium: a lossy, trained variant of "load fewer bytes for cold experts"; needs fine-tuned bit-width routers and offline quantization per model | read (full text): RTX 3060 laptop (6 GB) with an i7-11800H and 32 GB, and a Jetson AGX Orin 64 GB, NVMe disk; small MoE LLMs |
| 2025-04-23 | [PARD, arXiv 2504.18583](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18583) (5 auth.) | One small draft model, adapted at low cost, serves a whole model family (target-independent) and predicts several draft tokens in one parallel pass, instead of a separate trained EAGLE head per target | medium: a cheap parallel draft; a vLLM integration followed in May (PR #18541, above) | new (abstract) |
| 2025-04-08 | [Hogwild! Inference, arXiv 2504.06261](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06261) (9 auth.) | Several LLM "workers" of the same model run in parallel and see each other's tokens through a shared, concurrently updated attention cache, and decide themselves how to split a task | medium: a use of a shared KV cache for a multi-agent coding worker on one GPU; needs no training per the abstract, effect on small models not read | new (abstract) |
| 2025-04-24 | [The Sparse Frontier, arXiv 2504.17768](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17768) (6 auth.) | Largest study of training-free sparse attention: six methods, several model families, sequences to 128K, sparsity to 0.95 (1/20 of the attention budget), nine tasks; a taxonomy on four design axes and the finding that sparse attention is effective for long sequences but with task-dependent degradation | medium: the evidence base for any long-context sparsity at the 1M target; lossy, so a maintainer decision | new (abstract) |
| 2025-04-16 | [LLM inference on CPU-GPU coupled architectures, arXiv 2504.11750](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11750) (6 auth.) | Profiler-based analysis of loosely coupled (PCIe A100 and H100) and closely coupled (GH200) systems: GH200 gives 1.9 to 2.7x faster prefill for Llama 3.2-1B at large batch, but stays CPU-bound up to 4x larger batch sizes (kernel launch and queueing, Grace CPU) | medium: launch overhead decides small-batch decode, which is the reason for the persistent kernel; datacenter hardware | new (abstract) |
| 2025-04-16 | [BitNet b1.58 2B4T, arXiv 2504.12285](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12285) (8 auth.) | First open native 1.58-bit model at 2B parameters, 4T tokens, on par with full-precision open models of similar size on the benchmarks run, lower memory and decoding latency | medium: a model, not a technique; too small for our agent workloads | new (abstract) |
| 2025-04-04 | [Nemotron-H, arXiv 2504.03624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03624) | Family of hybrid Mamba-Transformer models | medium: an early large hybrid with SSM layers next to the DeltaNet line | new (title only) |

Notes (one line each, rows in the book): 2504.06319 (L2-cache KV prefetch, H20), 2504.06214 (continued pretraining to 1M, 2M, 4M context), 2504.05586 and 2504.07807 (expert dropping and pruning, lossy), 2504.18415 (BitNet v2, native 4-bit activations, needs training), 2504.16795 (hardware-aligned hierarchical sparse attention, needs training), 2504.14366 (what matters when linearizing models), 2504.04823 (quantization and reasoning), 2504.04104 (speculation inside pipeline parallelism), 2504.17376 (Qwen2.5 compression on an FPGA-class device), 2504.03083 (AMD NPU for training), 2504.16053 (LongMamba, training-free), 2504.20039 (AutoJudge, judge decoding without annotation), and a security paper on the Apple SLC cache (2504.13385, dropped).

## Backfill 2025-03 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2025-03-01 to 2025-03-31 of the backfill, scheme as in the Backfill 2026-07 section: all arXiv titles read, method per row in the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). Context for the whole Q1 2025 block: DeepSeek V3/R1 (MLA, MoE, MTP) dominates the serving repositories, Qwen3 and Gemma 3 land in the engines at the end of March, and RDNA4 (gfx1200/gfx1201) only just appears as a build target.

### arXiv 2025-03-01 to 2025-03-31

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 315 hits, complete. All 315 titles read; abstracts for the 13 that could matter; no full text (no row reaches `high`). Numbers are the authors'. 237 rows `drop` as `off-topic` (medical, vision, time series, federated, speech, graph and similar), 70 `note`, 8 `candidate`.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-03-02 | [DuoDecoding, arXiv 2503.00784](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00784) (4 auth.; code KaiLv69/DuoDecoding) | Draft model on the **CPU**, target on the GPU, drafting and verification in parallel; hardware-aware draft budget minimises idle time, dynamic multi-sequence drafting raises draft quality; up to 2.61x lower generation latency, TTFT 83 % of conventional speculative decoding | medium: the CPU as a second compute unit for the draft is the heterogeneous variant of our "draft from what is already resident" question; whether our CPU has the headroom next to the expert path is unchecked | read (abstract); hardware not checked |
| 2025-03-17 | [ML-SpecQD, arXiv 2503.13565](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13565) (4 auth.) | Plug-and-play MXFP4 direct-cast of the BF16 target as the draft (no draft training), then a second, smaller draft speeds up the MXFP4 draft itself (multi-level); up to 2x, with the multi-level scheme 2.72x over BF16 | medium: a draft made from the target's own weights is the lever; MXFP4 is not a gfx1200 compute format | read (abstract) |
| 2025-03-04 | [FlexInfer, arXiv 2503.03777](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03777) (5 auth.) | On-device offloading: asynchronous prefetch, balanced memory locking, flexible tensor preservation under a user-set memory budget; up to 12.5x over existing methods under tight memory | low: same family as our mmap and mlock policy for weights that exceed RAM; the 12.5x is against weak baselines | read (abstract) |
| 2025-03-10 | [eMoE, arXiv 2503.06823](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06823) (4 auth.) | Predicts the needed experts from recurrent routing patterns and loads only those; runs the predictor every few prompts; up to 80 % less memory, up to 17 % lower latency | low: the prediction idea for the VRAM expert cache; task-aware SLO scheduling is serving-side | read (abstract) |
| 2025-03-18 | [Tiled Flash Linear Attention, arXiv 2503.14376](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14376) (4 auth.) | Chunkwise-parallel linear-RNN kernels with an extra sequence-parallel level inside each chunk: arbitrary chunk size, higher arithmetic intensity, fewer materialised intermediate states; mLSTM kernels faster than Flash Attention, FLA and Mamba kernels | medium: the same chunk-size and state-materialisation trade as our DeltaNet chunk work; for xLSTM, not gated DeltaNet | read (abstract); kernels for NVIDIA |
| 2025-03-03 | [EAGLE-3, arXiv 2503.01840](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01840) (4 auth.; code SafeAILab/EAGLE) | Drops feature prediction for direct token prediction and fuses multi-layer features (training-time test); up to 6.5x, 1.4x over EAGLE-2, 1.38x throughput at batch 64 in SGLang | medium: the drafter family that the 2026 items build on; needs a trained drafter per target | read (abstract) |
| 2025-03-20 | [Mixture of Lookup Experts, arXiv 2503.15798](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15798) (7 auth.) | Experts re-parameterised as lookup tables keyed by input token id, stored off-GPU; no expert compute at inference | low: needs a model trained for it, cannot be applied to our existing checkpoints | read (abstract) |
| 2025-03-24 | [FFN Fusion, arXiv 2503.18908](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18908) (18 auth.) | Runs sequences of FFN layers (left after dropping attention layers) in parallel; Llama-3.1-405B to a 253B model, 1.71x lower latency | low: model surgery plus retraining, datacenter scale | read (abstract) |

| 2025-02-18 | [HeadInfer, arXiv 2502.12574](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12574) (10 auth.) | KV cache offloaded to CPU RAM with **head-wise** granularity (no layer's KV cache is ever complete on the GPU, attention computed per head); Llama-3-8B at 1M tokens: KV cache 128 GB to 1 GB, total GPU memory 207 GB to 17 GB; 4M tokens on one RTX 4090 24 GB without approximation | medium: the lossless KV-offload design for contexts far beyond VRAM (our 1M-context target); per-head transfers over PCIe set the decode rate (not in the abstract) | read (abstract) |
| 2025-02-13 | [InfiniteHiP, arXiv 2502.08910](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08910) (4 auth.) | Modular hierarchical token pruning (training-free) plus selective RoPE adjustment, KV cache offloaded to host memory; 3M tokens on one L40s 48 GB, 18.95x faster attention decode at 1M context; implemented in SGLang | medium: pruning plus host KV for very long contexts; pruning is approximate (attention to a subset), unlike HeadInfer | read (abstract) |
| 2025-02-10 | [Exploiting Sparsity for Long Context Inference, arXiv 2502.06766](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06766) (9 auth.) | Top-k attention at every step: up to 1M tokens in about 16 GB of GPU memory; attending to under 2 % of the tokens keeps over 95 % of benchmark quality (RULER, AlpacaEval, Open LLM Leaderboard) | medium: a 16 GB-class number for million-token contexts; lossy, quality check needed before use here | read (abstract) |

## Backfill 2025-01 (tool scan, complete pagination)

Gap closure 2025-01-01 to 2025-01-31 of the backfill, scheme as in the Backfill 2025-03 section: all arXiv titles read, method per row in the [screened book](papers-screened.tsv). Context: the month of DeepSeek V3 and R1; the first MLA implementations and the first expert-placement controls appear in llama.cpp, and the serving engines add EAGLE, MLA and AMD paths.

### arXiv 2025-01-01 to 2025-01-31

`scan.py arxiv` (topics v1, 7-day windows, paginated to `totalResults`): 214 hits, complete; 3 of them already stood in this file. The month is thin: 14 of the topic windows return 0 hits (moe-offload in four of the five windows, for example); a repeat of the speculative, moe-offload and amd-rocm topics for 2025-01-01 to 01-14 gave the same counts, so these are real zeros, not empty API pages. All 214 titles read; abstracts for the 9 that could matter; no full text (no row reaches `high`). Numbers are the authors'. 172 rows `drop` as `off-topic`, 35 `note`, 7 `candidate`.

| Date | Paper | Key statement | Relevance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-01-14 | [MiniMax-01, arXiv 2501.08313](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08313) (90 auth.; weights released) | Hybrid of lightning (linear) attention and softmax attention with a 32-expert MoE (456B total, 45.9B active); 1M-token context in training, 4M at inference | medium: an early large hybrid linear-attention MoE with million-token context, the family our DeltaNet hybrids belong to; far beyond 16 GB | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-03 | [CAPTURE, arXiv 2501.01792](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.01792) (6 auth.) | Single-GPU offloading of weights and KV cache: stores activation checkpoints instead of keys and values (half the capacity) and recomputes on idle GPU time, with the KV-to-activation ratio chosen to balance recompute time and transfer time; 2.19x throughput over the prior offloading system | low: throughput-oriented (batched), the recompute-versus-transfer balance is the general idea for a PCIe-limited link | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-31 | [Lossless speculative decoding for heterogeneous vocabularies, arXiv 2502.05202](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05202) (8 auth.) | Three lossless methods that let any off-the-shelf small model draft for any target regardless of tokenizer; up to 2.8x on summarization, code and long context | low: the lossless counterpart to the text-round-trip approach in llama.cpp #12635 (see 2025-03) | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-02 | [FlashInfer, arXiv 2501.01005](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.01005) (11 auth.) | Attention engine: block-sparse and composable KV formats, JIT-compiled customizable templates, load-balanced scheduling compatible with CUDA Graph; 29 to 69 % lower inter-token latency than compiler backends, 28 to 30 % for long context; used in SGLang, vLLM, MLC-Engine | low: NVIDIA kernels for batched serving; the load-balanced split of attention work over a fixed grid is the persistent-kernel idea | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-11 | [Characterizing Mobile SoC for heterogeneous LLM inference (HeteroInfer), arXiv 2501.14794](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14794) (8 auth.) | GPU and NPU run one model in parallel on a unified-memory SoC, with a fast synchronization mechanism exploiting the unified memory; 1.34x to 6.02x over GPU-only and NPU-only engines | low: phone SoC; the cross-processor synchronization on unified memory is the part that carries over to Apple silicon | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-13 | [FlexQuant, arXiv 2501.07139](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07139) (4 auth.) | Elastic quantization for unified-memory edge devices where available memory fluctuates: an ensemble of quantized models from one pruning scheme, 31x more deployment options, 15x finer granularity, 10x less storage than prior methods | low: the memory-elasticity idea matches our "work first" memory policy, but we elasticize the VRAM and RAM split, not the bit width | read (abstract) |
| 2025-01-22 | [GANQ, arXiv 2501.12956](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12956) (2 auth.) | Training-free layer-wise non-uniform quantization built for lookup-table mpGEMM, 3 and 4 bit; up to 2.57x speed-up on an RTX 4090 | low: lookup-table dequantization kernels for NVIDIA; the table lookup is the same trick as the IQ formats | read (abstract) |

Notes (abstract or title only, rows in the book): 2501.09398 (kernel batching with CUDA graphs: an optimal batch size balances graph creation against launch savings), 2501.14925 (Apple silicon unified memory for training, attributed to page faults and kernel launch time), speculative variants (2501.19309, 2501.19324), 2501.15665 (StagFormer, needs training), quantization methods (2501.01144, 2501.04052 NVFP4, 2501.13331, 2501.13987, 2501.13484 for Mamba), 2501.10132 and 2501.02342 (function-calling benchmark and small models), KV-cache compression (2501.19392, 2501.15021, 2501.15113, 2501.04987, lossy), datacenter serving (2501.12162, 2501.14312, 2501.14417).

### GitHub 2025-01-01 to 2025-01-31 (`scan.py github`, complete pagination, window splitting)

Six repositories carry PRs (the ROCm systems repositories and mlx-lm did not exist yet). 1290 PRs (vLLM 581, SGLang 383, llama.cpp 247, mlx 49, TheRock 21, ggml 9; every one has a row in the screened book). Screening depth, honestly: **llama.cpp, ggml and MLX: every PR title read** (`title-read`; bodies read for the rows marked; CI, docs, SYCL, CANN, webui and sync titles were skimmed as such); **vLLM, SGLang and TheRock were keyword-filtered** (the 829 PRs outside the filter are `keyword-filter`). Merged status from the GitHub API.
